`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`:

```md
# Architecture

Package layout, components and the professional RASP features.

## Feature overview

| Feature | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Runtime self-integrity | `rasp_self.c`, code `610` / `306` | At start-up it records the SHA-256 of the app's `__text` and the first 32 bytes of about 40 sensitive functions (libsystem and the RASP core). Every evaluation compares them again. Any code patched in memory after launch (Frida `Interceptor`, late Substrate or ElleKit hooks, breakpoints) is reported, even when the patch uses an encoding the prologue decoder does not recognise. |
| Static prologue and GOT checks | `rasp_hooks.c` | Trampoline decoding, fishhook and interposing detection, swizzle checks (since v1.0). |
| Scattered inline guard | `IntegrityGuard` / `rasp_quick_guard()` | A header-only, `always_inline` check using raw syscalls only (P_TRACED, CS_DEBUGGED, `/var/jb`). Call `IntegrityGuard.isClean` at many sensitive points so there is no single function to hook. |
| Check-completion bookkeeping | `IntegrityKit` | Each check sets "started" and "completed" bits, and missing bits raise `checkSuppressed`. Checks run in random order. |
| Tamper-evident result | `ProtectedVerdict` (`kit.lastVerdict`) | The verdict is held XOR-masked with an HMAC under per-process random keys. A patched value reads as `compromised`. |
| String obfuscation | `Scripts/gen_strings.py` | Every indicator string is XOR-encoded with a per-string rolling key and wiped after use. No indicators appear in plaintext in the binary. |
| Build hardening | `Apps/Hardening.xcconfig` | Strip all symbols, private-extern C symbols, `-Os`/`-O`, whole-module optimisation, LTO, dead-code stripping. See [Build hardening](INTEGRATION.md#build-hardening). |
| Signed remote rules | `DetectionRules.swift`, `Server/rules_tool.py` | Lets you add jailbreak paths, image markers, URL schemes and Frida ports, and tune weights and thresholds within bounds, without an app release. Rules are ECDSA-P256-signed, versioned with anti-rollback, have an expiry, and are additive only. See [Remote rules](INTEGRATION.md#remote-rules). |
| Reference server | `Server/` | Verifies App Attest attestations and assertions, the Secure Enclave seal and challenge freshness, re-scores signals with its own weights and returns `allow`, `step_up` or `deny`. It also serves the signed rules. There are 9 offline tests. |
| Lab harness | `Lab/` | A jailbroken-device checklist, expected signals per scenario, in-app "Copy result JSON" and `compare_results.py`. |

## Architecture

```
Kaisel/
├── Package.swift                      SwiftPM manifest, iOS 15+, product: KaiselKit
├── Sources/CIntegrityCore/            C core: evidence only, no policy
│   ├── include/rasp_core.h            public C API, finding codes, inline guard
│   ├── rasp_internal.h                force-inlined raw `svc #0x80` syscalls
│   ├── rasp_strings_table.h           GENERATED, no plaintext indicators
│   ├── rasp_fs.c                      artefacts, errno oracle, SSV, sandbox, canaries
│   ├── rasp_images.c                  dyld images (two sources), interposing, runtime monitor
│   ├── rasp_hooks.c                   inline hooks, GOT rebinding, stub integrity, swizzling
│   ├── rasp_debug.c                   P_TRACED, CS_DEBUGGED, exception ports, ppid, Developer Mode
│   ├── rasp_frida.c                   threads, loopback D-Bus probe, anonymous exec memory
│   ├── rasp_codesign.c                csops flags, entitlements blob, cryptid, __TEXT hash
│   ├── rasp_env.c                     DYLD_* / jailbreak variables, getenv cross-check
│   ├── rasp_self.c                    __text hash and prologue snapshots (self-integrity)
│   └── rasp_rules.c                   extra indicators from signed rules
├── Sources/KaiselKit/                 Swift: policy, scoring, sealing
│   ├── IntegrityKit.swift             entry point, orchestration, tamper bookkeeping
│   ├── JailbreakDetector.swift        jailbreak-only API used by Kaisel JB Check
│   ├── Finding.swift                  codes, domains, weights, hard indicators
│   ├── RiskScorer.swift               damped noisy-OR scoring per domain
│   ├── Report.swift                   Signal, IntegrityReport, posture and build facts
│   ├── Configuration.swift            distribution profile, expectations, overrides
│   ├── IntegrityGuard.swift           inline guard and tamper-evident verdict
│   ├── DetectionRules.swift           signed remote rules (verify, store, apply)
│   ├── Attestation.swift              App Attest, Secure Enclave, DeviceCheck, SealedReport
│   ├── IntegrityMonitor.swift         jittered periodic re-evaluation
│   ├── PlatformCapabilities.swift     per-version observability notes
│   └── Checks/                        C bridge, app-integrity and posture probes
├── Tests/KaiselKitTests/              scorer, policy and hardening unit tests
├── Apps/                              XcodeGen spec for the two apps
│   ├── project.yml                    schemes: KaiselJBCheck, KaiselIntegrityCheck(+Attest)
│   ├── KaiselJBCheck/                 Kaisel JB Check (SwiftUI)
│   ├── KaiselIntegrityCheck/          Kaisel Integrity Check (SwiftUI)
│   └── Hardening.xcconfig             Release hardening settings
├── Server/                            Python reference server (attestation, policy, rules)
├── Lab/                               jailbroken-device test plan and result comparison
├── Scripts/                           IPA builds, string obfuscation, icon generator
├── Branding/                          icon masters and preview
└── docs/                              documentation
```

The C layer reports findings (`code`, `confidence`, `detail`) and sets a "started" and a "completed" bit per check. The Swift layer applies the configuration, weights, scoring and sealing.

```

`docs/BUILDING.md`:

```md
# Building

Kaisel builds with Xcode on macOS. The Xcode project is generated from
[`Apps/project.yml`](../Apps/project.yml) by XcodeGen, so it is not committed.

## Requirements

| Requirement | Version | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| macOS | 14 Sonoma or later | |
| Xcode | 15.0 or later (Swift 5.9) | iOS SDK 17+; the apps deploy to iOS 15.0 |
| XcodeGen | 2.38 or later | `brew install xcodegen` |
| iPhone or iPad | iOS 15.0 to 27 | Detection only means something on a real device |
| Apple ID | free or paid | Free works for both apps; App Attest needs a paid team |
| Python | 3.10+ (optional) | Only for the reference server, the lab tools and the icon script |

## Schemes

| Scheme | App name on the phone | What it is | Apple account |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KaiselJBCheck` | Kaisel JB Check | Jailbreak-only verdict with evidence | Free or paid |
| `KaiselIntegrityCheck` | Kaisel Integrity Check | Full kit: device, runtime and app verdicts, posture, Secure Enclave seal | Free or paid |
| `KaiselIntegrityCheckAttest` | Kaisel Integrity Attest | Integrity Check plus App Attest enrolment and assertions | **Paid only** |

## Option A: Xcode (run directly on your phone)

```bash
git clone https://github.com/0xdhanesh/Kaisel.git
cd Kaisel/Apps
xcodegen generate
open Kaisel.xcodeproj
```

1. Select the `KaiselJBCheck` or `KaiselIntegrityCheck` scheme.
2. In **Signing & Capabilities**, choose your team. A free Apple ID appears as "Your Name (Personal Team)".
3. If Xcode says the bundle identifier is unavailable, change `KAISEL_BUNDLE_PREFIX` in `Apps/project.yml`
   (for example `com.yourname.kaisel`), then run `xcodegen generate` again.
4. Connect the phone, select it as the run destination and press **Run**.
5. On iOS 16 or later, enable **Settings › Privacy & Security › Developer Mode** when asked, and restart.
6. With a free Apple ID, trust the developer once: **Settings › General › VPN & Device Management**.

## Option B: Signed IPA from the command line

```bash
Scripts/build_ipa.sh --team ABCDE12345                                  # Kaisel JB Check
Scripts/build_ipa.sh --team ABCDE12345 --scheme KaiselIntegrityCheck
Scripts/build_ipa.sh --team ABCDE12345 --scheme KaiselIntegrityCheck --bundle-prefix com.yourname.kaisel
```

Your Team ID is in Xcode › Settings › Accounts, or on developer.apple.com under Membership.
The IPA is written to `build/ipa/<Scheme>.ipa`. Install it with Xcode's **Devices and Simulators**
window (drag the IPA onto the device), Apple Configurator, or `xcrun devicectl device install app`.

`--method ad-hoc` needs a paid team and the device UDIDs registered in your account.

## Option C: Unsigned IPA for sideloading tools

```bash
Scripts/build_unsigned_ipa.sh                   # both apps
Scripts/build_unsigned_ipa.sh KaiselJBCheck     # one app
```

This writes `dist/<Scheme>-<version>-unsigned.ipa` and `dist/SHA256SUMS.txt`. No developer account
is needed to build. Install with Impactor or Sideloadly, which sign the app with your Apple ID:
see [Sideloading](SIDELOADING.md). Tagged releases on GitHub publish the same files.

## Common build errors

| Error | Fix |
|---|---|
| `Personal development teams ... do not support the App Attest capability` | You built `KaiselIntegrityCheckAttest` with a free account. Build `KaiselIntegrityCheck` instead. |
| `Failed to register bundle identifier` / `is not available` | Another account already owns the ID. Set your own `KAISEL_BUNDLE_PREFIX`. |
| `The compiler is unable to type-check this expression in reasonable time` | Report it with the file and line; it is fixed by splitting the expression. |
| `No such module 'KaiselKit'` | Run `xcodegen generate` from `Apps/`, then **File › Packages › Reset Package Caches**. |
| App closes immediately after a free-account install | The 7-day profile expired or the developer is not trusted. Reinstall and trust it again. |

## Swift package only

```bash
xcodebuild -scheme KaiselKit -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' build
xcodebuild -scheme KaiselKit -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=<an installed iPhone simulator>' test
```

`swift build` on macOS will not work, because the package targets iOS only.

## Regenerating generated files

| File | Command |
|---|---|
| `Sources/CIntegrityCore/rasp_strings_table.h`, `Sources/KaiselKit/ObfuscatedLists.swift` | `python3 Scripts/gen_strings.py` |
| App icons and `Branding/` | `pip install Pillow && python3 Scripts/make_kaisel_icons.py` |
| `Server/weights.json` | `python3 Server/tools/export_weights.py` |

```

`docs/DETECTION.md`:

```md
# Detection

How Kaisel decides whether a device is jailbroken or a process is instrumented, and how the evidence is scored.

## How a jailbreak is decided

A stock iPhone has a few hard guarantees. The system volume is a sealed, read-only snapshot. Every app runs in a sandbox that cannot write outside its container or call `fork()`. Only Apple-signed system libraries and the app's own frameworks are loaded into the app. A jailbreak has to break at least one of these guarantees to do anything useful, and `JailbreakDetector` tests each one directly.

**Jailbroken** means at least one conclusive finding, that is, something a stock, sealed iOS cannot produce:

- **Jailbreak files visible to the kernel.** The rootless jailbreak root `/var/jb` (Dopamine, palera1n), or bootstrap and package-manager files. These are looked up with raw kernel syscalls, so tweaks that hook libc to hide files do not affect the result.
- **The root volume has been altered.** It is writable or remounted, or system directories have been replaced with symlinks (rootful jailbreaks).
- **The app sandbox is broken.** The app can write outside its container, or `fork()` succeeds.
- **A known jailbreak injector is loaded into the app.** Substrate, ElleKit, TweakInject, Dopamine's `systemhook`, RootHide's `roothideinit.dylib` and similar, matched by exact file name outside the app bundle, or any image loaded from a jailbreak root (`/var/jb/`, a RootHide `.jbroot-*` directory, `MobileSubstrate/DynamicLibraries`). Since 1.4.0 a name that merely contains a broad marker is never conclusive (see *Injection and loaded images*).
- **Jailbreak-only environment variables or entitlements.** For example `JB_ROOT_PATH`, or `no-sandbox`/`platform-application`, which only a TrollStore or jailbreak install can grant.
- **A hiding tweak is caught lying.** libc says a file does not exist while the kernel says it does, or dyld's internal image list has more entries than the public dyld API reports.

**Suspicious** means only indirect evidence: package-manager URL schemes (Cydia, Sileo, Zebra, Filza), a path whose existence is inferred from `EPERM`, a library loaded from an unexpected location, hooks on file APIs, or a check that did not complete.

**Not jailbroken** means this process observed no conclusive and no indirect evidence. It is not proof that the device is stock: per-app hiding such as RootHide's Blacklist removes everything the app can see (a measured miss, see [Limitations](LIMITATIONS.md)).

Build and signing facts (Xcode or ad hoc install, `get-task-allow`, provisioning profile, FairPlay encryption) and Developer Mode are **not** jailbreak evidence, and Kaisel JB Check ignores them.

## Detection techniques

None of the checks is a plain path lookup. Each one either uses a primitive that the usual hooks do not reach, or cross-checks two views of the same fact so that **a hook betrays itself**.

### Filesystem and jailbreak artefacts (`rasp_fs.c`)

| Technique | Detail |
|---|---|
| Raw syscalls | `stat64`, `lstat64`, `access`, `open`, `readlink` and `statfs64` are issued with inline `svc #0x80` (x16 = syscall number, carry flag = error). The helper is `always_inline`, so every check has its own trap instruction and there is no single wrapper to hook. fishhook, Substrate, ElleKit and Frida `Interceptor` hooks on libc never see these calls. |
| libc and kernel disagreement | Every artefact path is also looked up through libc. Kernel says "exists" and libc says `ENOENT` gives `libcSyscallMismatch`, a hard indicator of a hiding tweak (Shadow, A-Bypass, Liberty-style libc hooks). The reverse (libc reports a path the kernel denies) is reported as well. |
| Errno existence oracle | `EPERM`/`EACCES` from a sandboxed lookup means the path exists. To avoid false positives from opaque directories, the parent is first calibrated with a random, non-existent sibling: the result is only trusted when the sibling gives `ENOENT`. |
| Rootless root | `readlink("/var/jb")` resolving into `/private/preboot/<hash>/…` (Dopamine, palera1n rootless). |
| Signed System Volume | Since iOS 15 `/` is a sealed, read-only APFS snapshot (`com.apple.os.update-…`). If `/` is not read-only (`MNT_RDONLY` clear), or is not mounted from the sealed snapshot, that points to a rootful jailbreak or fakefs. |
| Stashing | System directories such as `/Applications` and `/usr/share` that have been replaced with symlinks (legacy rootful). |
| Obfuscated indicators | About 180 indicator strings are XOR-encoded with a key per string (`Scripts/gen_strings.py`). They are decoded to the stack for one comparison and then wiped, so `strings` on the binary shows none of them (verified). |

### Sandbox integrity (`rasp_fs.c`)

- An `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` write to `/private/.<random>` through a raw `open`. It always fails inside a stock sandbox.
- An `open(O_DIRECTORY)` on another domain's preferences directory.
- A `fork()` probe, resolved through `dlsym` so the binary has no `fork` import. The child exits through a raw `exit` syscall. The App Sandbox denies `fork`.

### Control probes (canaries)

- A positive control: the app's own executable must be visible through both the raw syscall and libc.
- A negative control: a random path in the container's tmp directory must give `ENOENT` through both.

A blanket hook that makes everything look absent, or everything look present, fails these probes and raises `canaryFailed` instead of quietly producing a "clean" result.

### Injection and loaded images (`rasp_images.c`)

| Technique | Detail |
|---|---|
| Two independent image lists | List A comes from `_dyld_image_count`/`_dyld_get_image_name`, which hiding tweaks hook. List B is dyld's own `dyld_all_image_infos->infoArray`, found through `task_info(TASK_DYLD_INFO)` and read directly from memory. If B has more entries than A, the dyld API is being filtered (`imageListMismatch`, hard). |
| Explicit image classes (1.4.0, `rasp_classify.c`) | Every image path is normalised (`/private/var` to `/var`) and put in exactly one class, from lists in `Server/classification.json`: **jailbreak injector** (200, conclusive): exact injector basename outside the bundle, or a path under `/var/jb/`, `MobileSubstrate/DynamicLibraries/`, `/usr/lib/TweakInject/` or a `.jbroot-*` component; **instrumentation** (205, runtime): exact Frida/Cycript basenames, or an injector basename *inside* the bundle (a re-signed app with injected tweaks can run on a stock phone); **trusted**: system locations and the app's own bundle, compared on a directory boundary so `/var/App.app2` is not inside `/var/App.app`; **marker** (206, indicator): a broad substring such as `crane` or `liberty`, or a signed remote-rules marker, in the *file name* of an image outside the system and the bundle; **unknown external** (201, indicator): anything else outside the system and the bundle. |
| What changed in 1.4.0 | Up to 1.3.1 broad markers were matched against the whole path and any match that did not mention Frida was treated as a conclusive injector, so a framework called `CraneBank`, `LibertyFinance` or `ChoicyAnalytics` could make a stock phone "Jailbroken". Broad markers are now indicators and never apply to in-bundle images. Remote-rules markers can never create a conclusive verdict. In-bundle code is classified by identity only: a renamed library added to a re-signed bundle is not caught by this check (a build manifest is on the roadmap). |
| `__interpose` sections | A non-system image with `__DATA,__interpose` or `__DATA_CONST,__interpose` is a DYLD-interposing hook library. |
| Runtime monitor | A `_dyld_register_func_for_add_image` callback records every image loaded after start-up (for example a late `dlopen` of an agent). The ring buffer is drained on each evaluation; each untrusted image is reported with its class (200, 205, 206 or 201, same rule as at start-up) plus `imageAddedAtRuntime` (203, indicator). Before 1.4.0 a late image could be conclusive on confidence alone. |

### Hook integrity (`rasp_hooks.c`)

For about 26 sensitive functions (`stat`, `lstat`, `access`, `csops`, `__sysctl`, `open`, `fopen`, `getenv`, `sysctl*`, `ptrace`, `fork`, `dlopen`, `dlsym`, `dladdr`, `_dyld_*`, `task_info`, `task_threads`, `objc_msgSend`, `SecItemCopyMatching` and others):

| Check | What it decodes |
|---|---|
| Inline trampolines | `LDR Xt, #lit ; BR Xt` (Substrate, Frida, ElleKit, Dobby), `ADRP Xd ; … ; BR Xd`, and the `BRAAZ`/`BRABZ` variants. |
| Far branch | A `B imm26` at entry is followed, and if the target lies outside the function's own image (or in anonymous memory) it is reported. A legitimate tail branch inside the same image is not. |
| Software breakpoints | `BRK #imm` at entry. |
| Syscall stub integrity | For raw stubs (`stat`, `lstat`, `access`, `csops`, `getppid`, `__sysctl`, `readlink`), `svc #0x80` must appear in the first eight instructions. |
| GOT rebinding (fishhook, interposing) | A function's address taken through our own GOT (`&stat`) is compared with `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT, "stat")`, and the resolved image must be a system image. |
| Objective-C swizzling | The IMP of `-[NSFileManager fileExistsAtPath:]`, `-[UIApplication canOpenURL:]`, `-[NSProcessInfo environment]`, `-[NSBundle bundleIdentifier]` and others must live in Foundation or UIKitCore, and its prologue must be clean. Frida's `ObjC.implementation` and `MSHookMessageEx` both move it. |
| Self-integrity | The RASP's own C entry points are checked for trampolines. If a check is hooked to return early, its "started" bit is never set (see [Tamper resistance](#tamper-resistance)). |

PAC is stripped with `ptrauth_strip` before decoding, where it applies.

### Debugger (`rasp_debug.c`)

| Check | Detail |
|---|---|
| `P_TRACED` | `KERN_PROC_PID` read through a **raw** `__sysctl` (the libc `sysctl` is a common hook target). |
| `CS_DEBUGGED` | `csops(CS_OPS_STATUS)` through a raw syscall. The kernel sets the flag once a debugger has attached, and it stays set for the life of the process, so it survives a detach. |
| Exception ports | `task_get_exception_ports` for breakpoint, bad access, bad instruction, software and arithmetic exceptions. LLDB and Frida register these. A crash reporter may too, so the expected count is configurable. |
| Parent process | A raw `getppid() != 1`: the app was spawned by debugserver or `frida -f` rather than by launchd. |
| Optional countermeasure | `IntegrityKit.denyDebuggerAttach()` calls `PT_DENY_ATTACH` through a raw syscall. It is compiled out in `DEBUG` builds. |

### Frida and instrumentation (`rasp_frida.c`)

| Check | Detail |
|---|---|
| Thread names | `task_threads` plus `thread_info(THREAD_EXTENDED_INFO)` looking for `gum-js-loop`, `gmain`, `gdbus`, `pool-frida` and similar. Short generic names must match exactly. This catches FridaGadget on non-jailbroken devices too. |
| D-Bus handshake | A loopback TCP connect to the configured ports (27042 and 27043 by default), then a D-Bus SASL `\0AUTH\r\n`. Frida answers `REJECTED`, an unrelated service does not. An open port alone is a weak signal; the handshake is a hard one. |
| Anonymous executable memory | Walks the top-level VM map with `vm_region_recurse_64`, without descending into the shared-cache submap (which avoids stub-island false positives). An executable region that belongs to no dyld image and has no backing file (`proc_regionfilename`) is flagged. Frida maps its agent with its own Mach-O loader and allocates r-x trampoline pages for `Interceptor`. File-backed remaps such as `libobjc-trampolines` are ignored. |
| Memory markers | Flagged regions (16 MiB at most) are scanned for agent markers such as `frida_agent_main`, `GumScript` and `frida:rpc`. |

### Code signing and binary integrity (`rasp_codesign.c`, `AppIntegrityChecks.swift`)

| Check | Detail |
|---|---|
| `CS_VALID` | Cleared means the code signature has been invalidated at runtime. |
| `CS_GET_TASK_ALLOW` | Set in a non-development build means a re-signed or development-signed binary. |
| Entitlements blob | `csops(CS_OPS_ENTITLEMENTS_BLOB)`, falling back to the DER blob on newer kernels. It is searched for entitlements no App Store app can hold: `no-sandbox`, `platform-application`, `skip-library-validation`, `task_for_pid-allow`, `persona-mgmt` and others. This is the TrollStore and "ldid on a jailbroken device" fingerprint. |
| FairPlay `cryptid` | App Store and TestFlight binaries have `cryptid=1`. A dumped and re-signed IPA has `cryptid=0`. |
| `embedded.mobileprovision` | Must not be present in an App Store build. The profile's `TeamIdentifier` is compared with `expectedTeamIdentifier`, and `ProvisionsAllDevices` is flagged outside enterprise builds. |
| `__TEXT,__text` SHA-256 | Computed from the mapped (decrypted) pages for comparison with a build-time value, preferably on the server. |
| Bundle location and ID | The app must run from `/private/var/containers/Bundle/Application/`, and the bundle ID must match. |
| URL schemes | Jailbreak package managers and tools (Cydia, Sileo, Zebra, Filza, unc0ver, Activator). This needs `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` in the host app. **Audit rule:** a scheme is listed only if no stock Apple app can register it. TrollStore's `apple-magnifier` was removed after a false positive on a stock iOS 27 device, because the built-in Magnifier app owns that scheme. |

### Environment (`rasp_env.c`)

The environment block is read directly through `_NSGetEnviron()` and checked for `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES` and other `DYLD_*` variables, and for jailbreak variables (`_MSSafeMode`, `_SafeMode`, `JB_ROOT_PATH`, `JB_SANDBOX_EXTENSIONS` and others). Each hit is then cross-checked with `getenv()`. A variable that is present in the block but hidden from `getenv` is a hard tamper indicator.

### Developer Mode (`rasp_debug.c`, `AppIntegrityChecks.swift`)

There is **no public API** for Developer Mode. The kit uses three proxies and reports each one separately:

1. The sysctl `security.mac.amfi.developer_mode_status`, resolved by name and read through a raw `__sysctl`. If it is unreadable it gives `-1` (unknown), not `0`.
2. Inference: on iOS 16 and later a `get-task-allow` app only launches when Developer Mode is on. If we are running with `CS_GET_TASK_ALLOW`, Developer Mode is on.
3. A mounted Developer Disk Image: `debugserver` under `/Developer` (iOS 16 and earlier) or `/System/Developer` (iOS 17 and later, personalised DDI).

Developer Mode is reported in `posture` and as informational signals. It never affects a score or verdict.

### dyld integrity (`rasp_images.c`)

When dyld runs from `/usr/lib/dyld` itself, its code in memory should match the file. The check finds
dyld through `dyld_all_image_infos`, confirms the file has the same `LC_UUID`, then compares
`__TEXT,__text` using raw `open`/`pread`. It also checks the path dyld was loaded from (Apple-signed
`/usr/lib`, `/System` and cryptex locations allowed).

**Limits, learned the hard way.** On iOS 16 and later dyld normally re-executes from its copy inside the
dyld shared cache. That copy has the same UUID but a different code layout, so comparing it with the file
is meaningless: 1.3.0 did this and reported a stock iPhone18,1 on iOS 27.2.1 as **Jailbroken** (162,671
differing words). Since 1.3.1:
- dyld images flagged `MH_DYLIB_IN_CACHE` are skipped;
- only a small difference (1 to 64 words) is reported; anything larger is treated as "not the same code";
- differences that are only `brk` instructions (at most 8) are reported as a debugger breakpoint;
- `dyldModified` is an **indicator** (weight 0.50), never conclusive and never a hard indicator.

In practice the check only has a chance on iOS 15, where dyld runs from the file. It is unverified on a
jailbroken device.

### Userspace reboot (`rasp_fs.c`)

Dopamine restarts userspace after the kernel exploit. `kern.userspacereboottime` is then non-zero while
the kernel keeps its original boot time. Reported as the indicator `userspaceReboot` because stock iOS can
restart userspace too.

### Jailbreak memory (`JailbreakMemory.swift`)

When conclusive evidence is seen, a Keychain item records the first sighting. A later clean run reports
`previouslyJailbroken` as an indicator. Keychain items survive app deletion and per-app hiding, but not an
erase. The server-side per-key history is the tamper-resistant version.

The marker is written only for conclusive evidence with confidence 90 or more, never for an indicator.
It is ignored and deleted if its recorded evidence is no longer conclusive under the current rule.
Both apps show **Reset jailbreak history** while a marker exists, and host apps can call
`JailbreakHistory.reset()`. The protected verdict (`kit.lastVerdict`) is held in memory with per-process
keys and is never persisted, so it starts empty on every launch.

### One jailbreak rule for both apps

`JailbreakEvidence` decides what is conclusive: an explicit allow-list of finding codes, where an image
finding (200) counts only if its path still classifies as a known jailbreak injector. The same list is in
`Server/classification.json`, which the reference server loads; `Server/tests/test_parity.py` fails if
the Swift sets and the manifest differ. Stored history (the Keychain marker and the server's per-key
history) is re-validated with the same rule, so 1.3.x entries created by a broad-marker match are ignored.
`JailbreakDetector` uses it for the JB Check status, and
`IntegrityKit` uses it to set the **device** verdict to `compromised` when conclusive evidence appears in
any domain. A tweak injector such as `roothideinit.dylib` is scored in the runtime domain but proves the
device is jailbroken, so both apps now agree.

### Syscall stub check: reliability

`syscallStubAltered` (304) fires when a raw syscall wrapper (`csops`, `ptrace`, `stat` and others) has no
`svc #0x80` in its first 8 instructions. On a stock iOS 15 to 27 system these functions are direct stubs,
so a missing trap means someone rewrote the entry. In the RootHide lab run it fired only while tweaks were
injected and disappeared when the app was hidden, which is consistent with a real hook. It stays an
**indicator** (weight 0.70, confidence lowered to 60 in 1.3.0) and is not conclusive, because Apple can turn
a stub into a wrapper in a future release. Since 1.3.0 the check follows a same-image branch at the entry
before deciding, and records the first four instruction words in the detail so lab runs can be diagnosed.

## Risk scoring

Every signal belongs to one **domain**, and each domain gets its own score and verdict:

| Domain | Question | Examples | Scored? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `device` | Is the phone jailbroken, or its OS tampered with? | `/var/jb`, root volume writable, sandbox broken, jailbreak environment variables or entitlements, libc hiding | Always |
| `runtime` | Is this process being hooked, injected, instrumented or debugged? | Frida threads or D-Bus, inline hooks, injected libraries, `P_TRACED` | Always (debugger codes are informational when `debuggerExpected`) |
| `app` | Does this binary match the distribution you expect? | `cryptid=0`, provisioning profile, `get-task-allow`, team ID | Only when `expectedDistribution` is set; otherwise **informational** |
| `posture` | How is the phone configured? | Developer Mode, Developer Disk Image | **Never** (informational) |

`report.verdict` is the worst of the enforced domains. Within each domain:

```
effective weight  w = base_weight(code) × confidence / 100       (0 if informational)
per category      p_cat = 1 − Π_i (1 − w_i · 0.5^i)              (sorted by w, descending)
domain            p     = 1 − Π_cat (1 − p_cat),   score = round(100 · p)
```

- **Damping inside a category.** Forty jailbreak paths count as one strong piece of evidence, not forty.
- **Hard indicators.** One hard indicator at 75% confidence or more floors the verdict at `high`. Hard indicators in two categories make it `compromised`.
- **Corroboration.** Anonymous executable memory on its own is informational. It is scored only when Frida, hook or injection evidence appears in the same report (see the iOS 27 note under [Lab validation plan](LAB.md#lab-validation-plan)).
- **Verdicts.** `trusted` (below 15), `low` (below 35), `elevated` (below 60), `high` (below 85), `compromised` (85 or more).
- **Overrides.** `weightOverrides` and `suppressedCodes`.

## Tamper resistance

| Mechanism | Purpose |
|---|---|
| No boolean | There is no `isJailbroken()` to hook and force to `false`. The report is a list of signals plus a score. |
| Started and completed bitmask | Each C check sets a "started" bit on entry and a "completed" bit on exit. A check that was never entered (hooked or replaced) or that never finished raises `checkSuppressed` (hard). |
| Randomised order | Checks run in a shuffled order on each evaluation, so "patch the third call" does not work across runs. |
| Inlined syscalls | There is no shared syscall wrapper to hook. Each check has its own trap. |
| Self-prologue check | Trampolines on the RASP's own entry points are detected. |
| Obfuscated strings | There is no plaintext indicator list to harvest, and generic "hide these paths" lists do not line up with ours. |
| Control probes | Blanket hooks are detected instead of producing a clean result. |
| Sealing | `seal(_:serverNonce:)` hashes the canonical report JSON together with a server nonce. It then signs that hash with an **App Attest assertion** (which proves a genuine, unmodified app instance) and a **Secure Enclave P-256 key** (which proves device-key continuity). A modified report, a replayed report or a report from a re-signed app fails on the server. |
| Monitor | Jittered re-evaluation with `IntegrityMonitor`. A very long gap between runs is reported (`monitorIntervalStarved`). |

## iOS 15 to 27: what can be observed

| | 15.x | 16.x | 17.x | 18.x | 26 | 27 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Public jailbreaks (at time of writing) | Rootful: checkra1n/palera1n (A8–A11). Rootless: Dopamine (A12+), palera1n, XinaA15 | Dopamine up to 16.6.1 (A12+); palera1n (A8–A11) | palera1n on checkm8 devices that get 17; no public A12+ kernel jailbreak | checkm8-class iPads only | None known | None known |
| TrollStore | Yes (14.0–16.6.1, 16.7 RC, 17.0) | Up to 16.6.1 / 16.7 RC | 17.0 only | No | No | No |
| Developer Mode | Does not exist | Exists; sysctl readable (best effort); inference via get-task-allow | Same, and personalised DDI under `/System/Developer` | Same | Same | **Tested:** sysctl readable (`developer_mode_status=1` read on iPhone18,1) |
| Sealed root snapshot check | Applies | Applies | Applies | Applies | Applies | **Tested:** no false positive |
| Raw `svc` syscalls and libc cross-check | Work | Work | Work | Work | Validate on device | **Tested:** no false positive |
| `dyld_all_image_infos` and hook checks | dyld4 layout | Same | Same | Same | Validate on device | **Tested:** no false positive |
| Anonymous executable memory | Applies | Applies | Applies | Applies | Validate on device | One unexplained 48 KiB r-x region seen on a stock device, so this signal now needs corroboration |
| `apple-magnifier` URL scheme | Owned by the stock Magnifier app: removed from the probe list | | | | | |
| Realistic threat on non-jailbroken devices | Repackaged IPA + FridaGadget, Developer Mode debugging, enterprise and ad hoc re-signing | Same | Same | Same | Same | Same |

What a sandboxed app **cannot** observe on any version:

- Kernel patches. A kernel-level hider (for example a rootless jailbreak that filters vnode lookups for selected apps) defeats the raw-syscall checks, because the kernel itself lies. Only artefacts outside the filesystem (images, threads, hooks, entitlements, memory) and server-side attestation remain.
- Whether the bootloader or SEP has been exploited (checkm8) with no userland traces.
- Developer Mode with certainty. It is best effort on every version.
- Other processes. The app cannot list processes, so a frida-server on a non-default port with a clean injection is only visible through in-process artefacts.

`PlatformCapabilities.notes` is embedded in every report, so the server knows which absences of evidence mean something.

```

`docs/INTEGRATION.md`:

```md
# Integrating KaiselKit

Adding the Swift package to your own app, build hardening and signed remote rules.

## Integration

### 1. Add the package

In Xcode, choose **File › Add Package Dependencies…** and enter the repository URL, or add it to `Package.swift`:

```swift
.package(url: "https://github.com/0xdhanesh/Kaisel.git", from: "1.3.0")
// target dependency:
.product(name: "KaiselKit", package: "Kaisel")
```

You can also vendor the folder and use **Add Local…**. The package has no third-party dependencies.

### 2. Host app `Info.plist`

```xml
<key>LSApplicationQueriesSchemes</key>
<array>
  <string>cydia</string><string>sileo</string><string>zbra</string><string>filza</string>
  <string>undecimus</string><string>activator</string>
</array>
```

### 3. App Attest entitlement

Add the App Attest capability: `com.apple.developer.devicecheck.appattest-environment` set to `production` for release builds. This needs a paid developer team. Without the entitlement the kit still works: `attestKey` throws `IntegrityError.appAttestUnavailable`, `appAttestStatus` reports `notEnrolled`, and `seal` returns a report signed with the Secure Enclave key only, with `attestationError` set to `appattest: not enrolled`. Your server should then treat App Attest as absent.

### 4. Use it

```swift
import KaiselKit

let kit = IntegrityKit(configuration: .init(
    expectedDistribution: .appStore,             // nil = app-build signals informational
    expectedTeamIdentifier: "ABCDE12345",
    expectedBundleIdentifier: "com.example.app",
    allowedExceptionPorts: 1                    // e.g. your crash reporter
))

// Once per install: enrol the App Attest key with your server.
let enrolment = try await kit.attestKey(serverChallenge: challengeFromServer)

// Before a sensitive operation (login, payment, adding a payee):
let report = await kit.evaluate()
let sealed = try await kit.seal(report, serverNonce: nonceFromServer)
try await api.submitIntegrity(sealed)            // the server decides

// Optional: continuous monitoring.
let monitor = IntegrityMonitor(kit: kit, interval: 30, jitter: 10) { report in
    if report.verdict >= .high { /* re-seal and send */ }
}
monitor.start()
```

### 5. Regenerating the indicator table

Edit the lists in `Scripts/gen_strings.py`, then run `python3 Scripts/gen_strings.py`. Each run uses new random keys. Set `RASP_SEED` for reproducible output. Keep the order of the `MISC` list in step with the `RASP_MISC_*` enum in `rasp_internal.h`.

### 6. Jailbreak-only use

```swift
let result = await JailbreakDetector().check()
switch result.status {
case .jailbroken:    print("Jailbroken:", result.conclusive.map(\.detail))
case .suspicious:    print("Suspicious:", result.indicators.map(\.detail))
case .notJailbroken: print("Not jailbroken")
}
```

## Build hardening

`Apps/Hardening.xcconfig` is applied to Release builds of both apps. Copy the same settings into your app:

- `STRIP_STYLE = all`, `STRIP_INSTALLED_PRODUCT = YES`, `DEPLOYMENT_POSTPROCESSING = YES`, `STRIP_SWIFT_SYMBOLS = YES`. The shipped binary has no symbol table, so `rasp_check_*` names are not visible. Keep the dSYM for crash symbolication.
- `GCC_SYMBOLS_PRIVATE_EXTERN = YES`, `DEAD_CODE_STRIPPING = YES`, `LLVM_LTO = YES`, `SWIFT_COMPILATION_MODE = wholemodule`, optimised builds. These inline the raw-syscall helpers into their callers.

Limits:
- **Swift metadata stays visible.** Type names such as `IntegrityKit` remain readable to class-dump-style tools. `SWIFT_REFLECTION_METADATA_LEVEL = none` hides them but breaks `String(describing:)` and some SwiftUI and Mirror use. A commercial obfuscator (symbol renaming, control-flow flattening) is the next step if you need more.
- **SwiftPM sets its own flags.** It does not let a package set `-fvisibility=hidden` without unsafe flags. Stripping at link time removes the symbols from the final executable anyway.

## Remote rules

```bash
cd Server
python3 rules_tool.py genkey --out keys            # keep keys/rules_private.pem offline
# edit rules/rules.json (bump "version"), then:
python3 rules_tool.py sign --key keys/rules_private.pem rules/rules.json   # writes rules/rules.sig
```

In the app:

```swift
let store = try RulesStore(publicKeyPEM: pinnedRulesPublicKeyPEM)
try? await store.update(rulesURL: rulesJSON, signatureURL: rulesSig)    // background, e.g. at launch
let kit = IntegrityKit(configuration: cfg, rules: store.current)       // applies verified rules
```

Safety rules:
- **Signature.** The rules are checked against the pinned public key on every load, including from disk.
- **Anti-rollback.** The highest accepted `version` is stored in the Keychain, and older files are refused.
- **Expiry.** Rules stop applying after `expiresAt`.
- **Minimum SDK.** Rules are ignored by SDKs older than `minSdkVersion`.
- **Additive only.** Rules can never remove compiled-in indicators.
- **Bounded tuning.** Weights stay within 0.5x to 1.5x of the built-in value, and thresholds move by at most ±15 points.
- **Fail-safe.** Any failure keeps the previous valid rules, or none.
- **URL schemes.** A scheme added by rules only works if it is already listed in `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes`.

## App Store considerations

- **Required-reason APIs.** App Store Connect enforces these at submission, whatever the deployment target. The kit references `stat`/`lstat` (File Timestamp), `statfs` (Disk Space) and `sysctl`. Apple's approved reasons do not include a security or anti-tamper reason. `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` therefore declares **no** reasons for you. Decide with your compliance team what to declare in the host app's manifest, or remove the libc cross-checks (the raw-syscall versions have no symbol reference).
- **`canOpenURL`** needs the schemes listed. App Review has accepted this for fraud prevention, but justify it in the review notes.
- **Raw syscalls and `fork`.** These are not private API, but they are unusual. Banking and RASP vendors ship equivalent code. Keep the `fork` probe behind `allowForkProbe` if your review history makes it a concern.
- **Loopback probing** does not trigger the Local Network permission prompt.

```

`docs/LAB.md`:

```md
# Lab validation

How to validate detections on real jailbroken devices, and current test status.

## Lab validation plan

The step-by-step harness is in [`Lab/LAB_CHECKLIST.md`](../Lab/LAB_CHECKLIST.md). It covers the scenarios, how to export results from either app (**Copy result JSON**), the run record (`.meta.json`, template in [`Lab/templates/`](../Lab/templates/)), the file-name convention, and `python3 Lab/compare_results.py --coverage --require-metadata Lab/results/*.json`, which prints PASS, PARTIAL, FAIL, INVALID or NOT TESTED per scenario against `Lab/expected.json`. P2 measurement scenarios (Frida attach, spawn, gadget, renamed markers, late attach; palera1n; Shadow-class hiding; fresh versus previously seen hidden installs) are in [`Lab/SCENARIOS.md`](../Lab/SCENARIOS.md). What has actually been measured is in [`Lab/COVERAGE.md`](../Lab/COVERAGE.md).

The table below summarises the expectations. They are targets and hypotheses, not measurements.

Run these on authorised test devices only. Record the signals each scenario produces, and tune the weights from that data.

| Scenario | Expected signals |
|---|---|
| Stock device, App Store or TestFlight build | None, or only `developerModeEnabled` if it is on. |
| Xcode debug run | `ptraced`, `codeSigningDebugged`, `getTaskAllow`, `parentProcessUnexpected`, `developerMode*` (all discounted by `.development`). |
| Dopamine (iOS 15 or 16, A12+), no hiding | `rootlessJailbreakRoot`, `jailbreakPathPresent` for `/var/jb/*`, `suspiciousImage` (systemhook, libellekit, TweakLoader), `envJailbreak`, URL schemes. |
| Dopamine with a hiding tweak | `libcSyscallMismatch`, `imageListMismatch`, `symbolRebound`, `inlineHook` on `stat`/`access`, `objcSwizzled`. |
| palera1n rootful (A8–A11) | `rootVolumeWritable`/`rootVolumeNotSealed`, `/.procursus_strapped`, `/cores/binpack`. |
| frida-server and `frida -U -f` | `fridaThread`, `fridaDBus`, `parentProcessUnexpected`, `anonymousExecutableMemory`, `inlineHook` on instrumented functions, `exceptionPorts`. |
| Repackaged IPA with FridaGadget (no jailbreak) | `instrumentationImage` (205, gadget; `suspiciousImage` before 1.4.0), `fridaThread`, `binaryDecrypted`, `provisioningProfilePresent`, `teamIdentifierMismatch`, `getTaskAllow`, App Attest failure on the server. |
| TrollStore install | `suspiciousEntitlement` (device), `binaryDecrypted` (app). |
| **Tested:** stock iPhone18,1, iOS 27.2.1, Xcode install (v1.0) | v1.0 wrongly reported **Compromised 91**. The causes were build-context signals (`cryptid=0`, `get-task-allow`, provisioning profile), Developer Mode, the `apple-magnifier` false positive and one anonymous r-x region (`0x100fe4000+0xc000`). v1.1 made these informational or removed them. The jailbreak-only app then reported **Not jailbroken** on the same device. If an anonymous executable region still appears on a stock device, open a false-positive issue with the `tag=`/`share=` details shown in the signal. |

## Recorded runs

|---|---|---|---|

The earlier *Suspicious* result did not reproduce; no residual signal fired |

**Current status of the hidden case:** a miss. The latest screenshots show both apps clean with the app on RootHide's Blacklist, so treat the earlier *Suspicious* row as unconfirmed until result JSON from a hidden run shows which signal, if any, fires.

## Test status

| Version | What has been verified |
|---|---|
| 1.0 | Compiled in Xcode and ran on a stock iPhone18,1 (iOS 27.2.1). That run drove the false-positive fixes in 1.1. |
| 1.1 | Jailbreak-only app ran on the same device and reported **Not jailbroken**. |
| 1.4.0 (unreleased) | Image classification and strict server validation. Not run on a device. See [V1.4.0-VALIDATION.md](V1.4.0-VALIDATION.md) for the tests that ran and their results. |
| 1.3.1 | Fixes the 1.3.0 stock iOS 27 false positive. Server tests pass (14). |
| 1.3 | Server tests pass (12). C changes pass `clang -Wall -Wextra -fsyntax-only` against stub headers, including the arm64 paths. Swift changes are not compiled here; CI on GitHub builds them. |
| 1.2 | C core passes `clang -Wall -Wextra -fsyntax-only` (x86_64 and AArch64, stub headers). The reference server's 9 tests and an HTTP smoke test pass. The Swift changes in 1.2 (self-integrity, rules, App Attest opt-in, Kaisel rename) are verified by the CI build on GitHub, not yet on a device. |

**Only one jailbroken device so far** (see Recorded runs and [Lab/COVERAGE.md](../Lab/COVERAGE.md)). No result JSON has been exported for any run yet. Every other jailbreak, hiding-tweak and Frida expectation in
[`Lab/expected.json`](../Lab/expected.json) is a hypothesis until the [lab checklist](../Lab/LAB_CHECKLIST.md)
has been run. Results are welcome through the *Detection gap or lab result* issue template.
