`docs/LIMITATIONS.md`:

```md
# Limitations

What Kaisel cannot do, and what has not been proven yet.

## What is still unproven

Be clear about this before relying on the kit:

- **One jailbroken device so far.** Real-device runs are listed in [Lab/COVERAGE.md](../Lab/COVERAGE.md): Dopamine + RootHide on an iPhone9,3 (iOS 15.8.2), stock iPhone18,1 (iOS 27.2.1), a stock iOS 26 device and one TrollStore install. All are screenshots or owner reports; no result JSON has been exported. Every other jailbreak, hiding-tweak and Frida expectation in `Lab/expected.json` is a hypothesis.
- **1.4.0 has not been run on a device.** Its image-classification and server-validation changes are covered by unit tests only.
- **"Not jailbroken" is not proof.** It means no conclusive or indirect evidence was observed by this process. Per-app hiding is a measured miss (below), and no client-side check can prove the OS is unmodified.
- **In-bundle code is classified by identity only.** Since 1.4.0 images inside the app's bundle are trusted unless their exact name is a known instrumentation or injector library. A renamed library added to a re-signed bundle is not caught by image classification; App Attest, the team ID and the `__TEXT` hash are the backstops, and a build manifest is on the roadmap.
- **Server validation cannot prove the checks ran.** Completion bits are self-reported. App Attest proves the genuine app instance signed the report, not that measurements inside a compromised process are true (see [Server](SERVER.md#strict-and-lab-modes-140)).
- **Prologue decoding and stub checks** are heuristics. Apple can change the system stubs between releases.
- **Self-integrity cannot see anything patched before the baseline.** For example, a dylib constructor that hooks before the kit initialises. The static decoder, the server-side `__TEXT` hash comparison and App Attest are the backstops.
- **A determined attacker with code execution can bypass any on-device check.** That includes this kit's tamper evidence. App Attest verified on your server is the only part an attacker cannot forge without a genuine, unmodified app.
- **The reference server is a teaching implementation.** It has single-process JSON storage, no TLS and no authentication. Port the verification steps into your backend.

## Known bypasses and limits

The point of this list is honesty. Every item below has been publicly demonstrated against RASP products.

| Bypass | Effect on this kit | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| **Patching the binary** (NOP the call, force the score to 0) | Defeats all client-side logic. | App Attest assertion fails for a modified binary; `cryptid`, team ID and `__TEXT` hash all change. **This is why the server must decide.** |
| **Hooking Swift or C entry points** with Frida, so `evaluate()` returns a forged report | Defeats a local verdict. | The sealed report's App Attest assertion still needs the genuine app's key. A forged payload must be signed in-process, which is possible under Frida. Signals such as threads, hooks and memory in the same run usually expose Frida. Pair this with server-side behavioural fraud signals. |
| **Kernel-level hiding** (vnode filtering, patched `csops`) | Raw-syscall filesystem checks and `csops` lie. | Image list, hooks, threads, entitlements and memory checks still apply. Treat absence of filesystem evidence as weak. |
| **Hiding tweaks that clean up dyld state** (removing images from `infoArray` as well as the API) | Defeats the image-list comparison. | Anonymous executable memory, the prologue and GOT checks, and swizzle checks remain. |
| **Renamed Frida** (strongR-frida, custom builds with renamed threads and strings, random port) | Defeats thread-name, port and marker checks. | Anonymous executable memory, inline hooks on the functions it instruments, exception ports and `CS_DEBUGGED` remain. |
| **Frida in "stalker" or no-trampoline modes** | Fewer inline patches. | Thread and anonymous-memory signals remain. |
| **Hardware-breakpoint or watchpoint debugging** | No `BRK` in code. | `P_TRACED`, `CS_DEBUGGED`, exception ports and ppid remain. |
| **Debugger attached after the check** | A launch-time check misses it. | `IntegrityMonitor`, and evaluating right before each sensitive operation. `CS_DEBUGGED` is sticky. |
| **TrollStore install with crafted entitlements** | Detected through the entitlement blob, unless the attacker picks entitlements that are not on our list. | Extend the list; check `cryptid` and the team ID. |
| **Instrumentation without Frida** (Dobby, custom inline hooks, `DYLD_INSERT` on a jailbroken device) | Partially covered by the generic trampoline, GOT and image allow-list checks. | The allow-list is location based, so unknown names outside the bundle are still caught (201, an indicator). |
| **A legitimate framework whose name contains a broad marker** (for example `CraneBank`) | Up to 1.3.1 this was a conclusive "Jailbroken" (found by code review, not observed). | Fixed in 1.4.0: broad markers are indicators and never apply to in-bundle images. |
| **Simulator or emulation (Corellium)** | The simulator is detected. Virtualised iOS can look stock. | App Attest is not available on virtual devices: no assertion, no trust. |
| **False positives** | Crash reporters (exception ports), MDM or enterprise re-signing (profile, team), SDKs that swizzle `UIApplication`, the app suspended in the background (`monitorIntervalStarved`). | Use `allowedExceptionPorts`, the distribution profiles, `weightOverrides` and `suppressedCodes`. |

Prologue decoding is a heuristic. Apple can change the instruction sequences in system stubs between releases, so `syscallStubAltered` has a moderate weight. Validate it on every new major release.

## Per-app hiding (RootHide Blacklist, Choicy, Shadow)

**Tested:** on Dopamine + RootHide (iPhone9,3, iOS 15.8.2), adding the app to RootHide's Blacklist made
both apps report a clean device with zero signals (see the [lab record](../Lab/results/2026-11-21_iphone9-3_ios15.8.2_dopamine-roothide.md)).
The latest 1.3.x runs ([screenshots](../README.md#evidence-dopamine--roothide-iphone93-ios-1582)) show the same:
JB Check *Not jailbroken* and Integrity Check *Trusted 0*. The residual signals below did not fire, and an
earlier *Suspicious* reading on 1.3.1 did not reproduce, so the hidden case is currently a miss.
This is expected for a well-designed hider, and no in-app check can promise to beat it.

Why it works: the hidden app gets **nothing injected** (no hooks to detect), the jailbreak root lives at a
**random path** the sandbox cannot list or guess, there is **no `/var/jb`**, and the package managers'
URL schemes are filtered by the system service that answers `canOpenURL`. The app runs in a near-stock
process on a jailbroken kernel.

What can still be observed from inside a hidden app, and how good each is:

| Residual | Kaisel check | Reliability |
|---|---|---|
| dyld patched or loaded from a non-system path (jailbreaks that hook dyld for every process) | `dyldModified` (110), compares in-memory `__text` with `/usr/lib/dyld` | Indicator only. Meaningful only where dyld runs from the file (iOS 15); skipped when dyld runs from the shared cache (iOS 16+). 1.3.0 wrongly treated it as conclusive and flagged a stock iOS 27 phone. Unverified on RootHide. |
| Userspace reboot after the exploit | `userspaceReboot` (111), `kern.userspacereboottime` | Indicator only. Stock iOS can also restart userspace; the sysctl may be unreadable on some versions. Unverified. |
| The device was seen jailbroken earlier | `previouslyJailbroken` (908), Keychain marker | Indicator. Only works if the app ran unhidden at least once; a user can delete it. Markers whose evidence is no longer conclusive (such as 1.3.0's dyld false positive) are deleted on read. |
| Same hardware key seen jailbroken before | Reference server per-key history | Strong for the "run once, then hide" pattern; resets on erase or if the key is lost. |
| Kernel patches, trust-cache changes, PPL/KTRR bypass state | none | **Not observable** from the app sandbox. |
| Random jailbreak root (`.jbroot-<random>`) | none | **Not guessable**; directory listing is denied by the sandbox. |
| Timing differences (syscall latency, path lookup) | none | Rejected: too noisy across devices and iOS versions to use without many false positives. |
| Processes, mach services, launchd jobs | none | Not visible: process listing and arbitrary `mach-lookup` are denied to apps. |

**Practical guidance:** treat "not jailbroken" as *no evidence*, not as *proof of a stock device*. For
high-value flows, combine the client report with server-side state:
- **App Attest** proves the genuine app on real hardware (not a stock OS).
- **DeviceCheck two bits** let your server mark a device as "seen jailbroken" in a way that survives
  reinstall and app hiding; set it the first time any of your apps reports conclusive evidence.
- **Per-key history** in the reference server does the same per Secure Enclave or App Attest key.
- Behavioural fraud signals on the backend.

```

`docs/Pentesting iOS RASP.md`:

```md
# Pentesting iOS RASP

An adversary-centred methodology for assessing iOS jailbreak detection and runtime application self-protection (RASP), mapped to Kaisel's checks.

| | |
|---|---|
| Audience | Mobile pentesters, red teamers and the Kaisel maintainers |
| Scope | iOS 15 to 27, jailbroken and non-jailbroken devices, client and server |
| Kaisel version | 1.3.x (signal codes from `Sources/KaiselKit/Finding.swift`) |
| Status | Methodology. Kaisel coverage claims marked *unverified* have not yet been confirmed on a jailbroken device (see [LIMITATIONS.md](LIMITATIONS.md)) |

> **Use.** This document is for authorised testing under written scope and rules of engagement, and for hardening Kaisel. It describes techniques, tools and observations at methodology level. It deliberately contains no bypass scripts, evasion code or step-by-step weaponised procedures.

---

## Contents

1. [Threat model and ground rules](#1-threat-model-and-ground-rules)
2. [How pentesters approach RASP](#2-how-pentesters-approach-rasp)
3. [Layer 1: Hide what the app can see](#layer-1-hide-what-the-app-can-see)
4. [Layer 2: Hook or patch the checks](#layer-2-hook-or-patch-the-checks)
5. [Layer 3: Tamper with the app before it runs](#layer-3-tamper-with-the-app-before-it-runs)
6. [Layer 4: Compromise below the app](#layer-4-compromise-below-the-app)
7. [Layer 5: Lie to the server](#layer-5-lie-to-the-server)
8. [Tiered test plan](#8-tiered-test-plan)
9. [Results table template](#9-results-table-template)
10. [Reporting template](#10-reporting-template)
11. [Proposed Kaisel tweaks](#11-proposed-kaisel-tweaks)
12. [Standards mapping cheat sheet](#12-standards-mapping-cheat-sheet)
13. [References](#13-references)

---

## 1. Threat model and ground rules

### The adversary

| Profile | Capability | Typical goal |
|---|---|---|
| **Curious user** | Public jailbreak, off-the-shelf hiding tweak | Use the banking app on a jailbroken phone |
| **Pentester / researcher** | Jailbreak, Frida, objection, LLDB, disassembler, re-signing toolchain | Reach the business logic behind the RASP, prove bypassability |
| **Fraud operator** | Repackaged IPA, instrumented farm devices, scripted API clients, report replay | Automate account takeover, mule onboarding, transaction fraud |
| **Advanced actor** | Private kernel exploit, kernel-level hiding, custom instrumentation | Persistent, quiet access on a targeted device |

### The adversary's five layers

The adversary escalates from the cheapest layer to the most expensive. A good RASP forces them down the stack; a good backend makes the bottom layers irrelevant.

```
 5  Lie to the server        replay, forward, forge, attestation abuse
 4  Compromise below the app kernel-level falsification
 3  Tamper before run        repackage, inject, re-sign, decrypt
 2  Hook or patch checks     Frida, Substrate/ElleKit, binary patching, debugger
 1  Hide what app can see    per-app hiding, path renaming, mounts, URL scheme filtering
```

### Core truths a tester should state up front

- **Any on-device check can be bypassed by an attacker with code execution in the process.** RASP raises cost; it does not provide proof. MASVS-RESILIENCE is defence-in-depth, never a substitute for server-side controls ([OWASP MASVS](https://mas.owasp.org/MASVS/)).
- **Apple does not attest device integrity.** App Attest proves a genuine app instance on genuine Apple hardware, not a stock OS ([Apple: Establishing your app's integrity](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/devicecheck/establishing-your-app-s-integrity); [Apple Developer Forums 731968](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/731968)).
- **"Not jailbroken" means "no evidence".** Per-app hiding on a modern rootless jailbreak can produce a near-stock process; Kaisel's own RootHide lab run proved this (see [lab record](../Lab/results/2026-11-21_iphone9-3_ios15.8.2_dopamine-roothide.md)).
- **The finding is rarely "the jailbreak check was bypassed".** The finding is "the backend allowed a high-risk action from an environment it could not trust". Report the business impact.

---

## 2. How pentesters approach RASP

The approach below is the common shape across OWASP MASTG, consultancy methodologies (NCC Group, WithSecure, Guardsquare, Corellium, Appknox write-ups) and conference material. It is iterative: each step tells you which layer to try next.

| Phase | Activity | Output |
|---|---|---|
| **0. Scope** | Confirm target build (App Store, TestFlight, enterprise, debug), devices, iOS versions, whether server-side decisions are in scope, and rules for account and transaction testing | Signed RoE; test matrix |
| **1. Baseline** | Run on a stock device. Record what the app does with no adversary present: launch time, prompts, telemetry, network calls carrying integrity data | Negative control; false-positive record |
| **2. Recon (static)** | Decrypt or obtain the binary. Identify the protection: SDK names, symbols, imports (`ptrace`, `sysctl`, `dlsym`, `fork`, `_dyld_*`, `csops`), string tables, inline `svc #0x80`, obfuscation, framework list, App Attest/DeviceCheck use | Map of checks and where they are triggered |
| **3. Recon (dynamic)** | Trace the app on a jailbroken device to find when checks run and what they return; correlate with network traffic | Check timeline, reaction points (UI block, crash, silent report) |
| **4. Layer attempts** | Work down the five layers, cheapest first. Record which layer first produced a clean result | Bypass depth per check |
| **5. Reaction analysis** | For each detection, what does the app *do*? Exit, degrade, step-up, silent server flag? Is the reaction local (patchable) or server-side? | Response effectiveness |
| **6. Server validation** | Does the backend enforce anything? Replay, strip, modify or forge integrity evidence and observe the decision | The real finding |
| **7. Report** | Per check: depth of bypass, effort, tools, business impact, mapping | Findings with MASVS/MASTG/regulatory mapping |

**Rating the result.** Many teams grade resilience by *effort to bypass*, not by pass or fail alone:

| Grade | Meaning |
|---|---|
| R0 | No detection, or detection with no reaction |
| R1 | Defeated by an off-the-shelf tool or tweak with no tuning (objection, Shadow, RootHide Blacklist) |
| R2 | Defeated by generic hooking once the check is located (hours) |
| R3 | Needed custom work: renamed tooling, binary patching, multiple layered hooks (days) |
| R4 | Needed kernel-level capability, or not defeated within the time box |
| S | Server-side enforcement held regardless of client bypass |

A resilient design reaches **R3 or above on the client and S on the server**.

---

## Layer 1: Hide what the app can see

### What the adversary may do

Make the app's view of the environment look stock without touching the app's code.

| Technique | Detail | Public examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Per-app hiding (no injection)** | Exclude the target from tweak injection entirely, so there are no hooks to find; the jailbreak's files are unreachable from that app | RootHide Blacklist, Choicy, tweak-injection opt-out in Dopamine/ElleKit |
| **Randomised jailbreak root** | Replace a fixed `/var/jb` with a random directory the sandbox cannot list or guess | RootHide (`.jbroot-<random>`) |
| **libc/Foundation path hiding** | Hook `stat`, `access`, `open`, `fopen`, `NSFileManager`, `canOpenURL:` so artefacts return "not found" | Shadow, A-Bypass, Liberty Lite, FlyJB, Hestia, iHide (TrustedSec) |
| **URL-scheme filtering** | Hide package-manager schemes (`sileo://`, `zbra://`, `cydia://`, `filza://`) from `canOpenURL:` | Built into most hiders; RootHide filters at the system service level |
| **Environment scrubbing** | Remove `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `_MSSafeMode`, `JB_ROOT_PATH` from the environment or from `getenv` | Most hiders; injectors themselves |
| **Image-list filtering** | Remove tweak dylibs from `_dyld_image_count` / `_dyld_get_image_name` and, in better hiders, from dyld's internal `infoArray` | Shadow, A-Bypass, Liberty |
| **Mount and volume tricks** | Bind-mount or overlay paths, restore read-only state of `/`, hide a fakefs | Rootful-era hiders, bindfs-style mounts |
| **Sandbox restoration** | Ensure `fork()`, out-of-container writes and cross-domain reads fail as on stock | Rootless jailbreaks keep the sandbox intact by design |

### How pentesters assess it

| Item | Method |
|---|---|
| **Preconditions** | Jailbroken test device on a supported version (e.g. Dopamine on A12+/iOS 15–16.6.1, palera1n on checkm8 devices, Dopamine-RootHide variant); package manager; at least two hiders; app installed from the build under test |
| **Test cases** | L1-1 stock jailbreak, no hiding. L1-2 generic hider (Shadow-class) enabled for the app. L1-3 per-app injection opt-out (Choicy/RootHide Blacklist). L1-4 renamed artefacts: package manager and bootstrap under non-default names. L1-5 URL schemes filtered. L1-6 run once unhidden, then hidden (history test) |
| **Tools** | Dopamine, palera1n, RootHide, Shadow, Choicy, A-Bypass, Liberty Lite, iHide, Filza, Sileo/Zebra; objection `ios jailbreak disable` as a *quick-look* baseline; Frida tracing of file APIs to confirm what is queried |
| **Observe** | Which signals disappear per test case. Does the app query paths via libc or via raw syscalls (visible as `svc` in the binary; invisible to libc hooks)? Does it cross-check views? Does the server see a difference between L1-1 and L1-3 runs from the same device? |
| **Pass** | Detection survives L1-2 (generic hider) and either survives L1-3 or the server flags the device from history (L1-6) |
| **Fail** | Any off-the-shelf hider yields a clean verdict and the backend accepts the session with no step-up |
| **Report** | "Jailbreak detection bypassed using off-the-shelf hiding tweak (R1)". Map: MASVS-RESILIENCE-1; MASWE-0051; legacy MASTG-TEST-0088 / MASTG-KNOW-0084; CBUAE RASP/MTD expectation (see §12); MITRE ATT&CK Mobile T1628 Hide Artifacts |

### What Kaisel provides today

| Adversary move | Kaisel check (code) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| libc path hiding | Raw `svc #0x80` lookups; **`libcSyscallMismatch` (108, hard)** when libc and kernel disagree | Inline per check, no shared wrapper. Defeats Shadow-class libc hooks by construction |
| Blanket "everything absent" hook | **`canaryFailed` (109)** positive/negative control probes | |
| Fixed jailbreak paths | `jailbreakPathPresent` (100), `rootlessJailbreakRoot` (105, `/var/jb` → `/private/preboot/…`) | ~180 obfuscated indicator strings |
| Opaque directories | `jailbreakPathErrnoOracle` (101) with sibling calibration | |
| Rootful remount, fakefs | `rootVolumeNotSealed` (103), `rootVolumeWritable` (104), `symlinkedSystemDirectory` (102) | Sealed snapshot check |
| Sandbox restoration | `sandboxWrite` (120), `sandboxFork` (121), `sandboxRead` (122) | Rootless jailbreaks usually pass these, as expected |
| URL schemes | `jailbreakURLScheme` (905) | Indicator only; RootHide filters it |
| Environment scrubbing | `envJailbreak` (701), `envGetenvMismatch` (702, hard) via `_NSGetEnviron()` vs `getenv` | |
| Image-list filtering | **`imageListMismatch` (202, hard)**: dyld API vs `dyld_all_image_infos->infoArray`; `unexpectedImagePath` (201) location allow-list | |
| Run unhidden, then hidden | `previouslyJailbroken` (908) Keychain marker; reference server per-key history | Only residual on 1.2 after RootHide Blacklist; 1.3.1 hidden run reports Suspicious |
| Residual jailbreak state | `dyldModified` (110), `userspaceReboot` (111) | Indicators; *unverified* on RootHide |

### Gaps

- **Per-app hiding with no injection defeats every filesystem and image check** (lab-confirmed, RootHide Blacklist, iPhone9,3 iOS 15.8.2). On 1.3.1 the hidden run reports **Suspicious** from residual indicators rather than clean, which is still not conclusive.
- **No mount-table view.** Kaisel checks `statfs("/")` but does not enumerate all mounts, so overlays and bind mounts elsewhere are invisible.
- **No probe of jailbreak-relaxed kernel policy** visible from the sandbox (code-signing flags beyond `CS_VALID`/`CS_GET_TASK_ALLOW`, sandbox extension behaviour).
- **History depends on one unhidden run**, and the Keychain marker is user-deletable. DeviceCheck bits are documented but not implemented in the reference server.

### Suggested tweaks (later)

See [§11](#11-proposed-kaisel-tweaks): **K1** mount table enumeration, **K2** full code-signing flag profile, **K3** DeviceCheck bits in the server, **K4** sandbox-extension and MAC policy probes.

---

## Layer 2: Hook or patch the checks

### What the adversary may do

Leave the environment alone and make the checks themselves lie.

| Technique | Detail | Public examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Boolean / return-value hooking** | Find the check function (e.g. `isJailbroken`, `amIJailbroken`) and force the result | Frida `Interceptor`, objection, Corellium and Appknox write-ups; Guardsquare scenario 1 against IOSSecuritySuite |
| **Low-level API hooking** | Hook `stat`, `fopen`, `dlsym`, `sysctl`, `ptrace`, `getppid`, `csops` and `_dyld_*` below the check | Frida, Substrate/ElleKit, fishhook, Dobby |
| **Objective-C swizzling** | Replace IMPs of `NSFileManager`, `UIApplication canOpenURL:`, `NSProcessInfo` | Frida `ObjC.implementation`, `MSHookMessageEx` |
| **Code tracing without patches** | Execute a transformed copy of the code so the original stays byte-identical, defeating prologue checks | Frida Stalker (Guardsquare scenario 2) |
| **Static binary patching** | NOP the call, invert a branch, force the score to zero, then re-sign | Ghidra/IDA/Hopper patching write-ups (e.g. "No Escape" lab) |
| **Debugger-driven bypass** | Breakpoint on the check and change registers, or use hardware breakpoints/watchpoints so no `BRK` is written | LLDB/debugserver (Diverto 2024), MASTG-TECH-0093 "Waiting for the debugger" |
| **Renamed instrumentation** | Rebuild Frida with renamed threads, strings, ports and agent names | strongR-frida, hluda, fridare |
| **Anti-anti-debug** | Neutralise `PT_DENY_ATTACH`, `sysctl` `P_TRACED`, exception-port and ppid checks | Common objection/Frida snippets, LLDB scripting |
| **Hook early** | Load before the RASP initialises (dylib constructor, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, spawn-time agent) so baselines are taken after hooks are in place | `frida -f`, tweak injection |

### How pentesters assess it

| Item | Method |
|---|---|
| **Preconditions** | Jailbroken device plus a non-jailbroken device with a re-signed, Gadget-injected build (Layer 3); disassembler; Frida (stock and renamed builds); LLDB with debugserver |
| **Test cases** | L2-1 objection generic bypass. L2-2 hook the public/high-level check entry point. L2-3 hook low-level libc/syscall wrappers. L2-4 swizzle Foundation/UIKit methods. L2-5 Stalker-style trace with no inline patches. L2-6 static patch of check or reaction. L2-7 LLDB with software breakpoints, then hardware breakpoints. L2-8 renamed Frida on non-default port. L2-9 spawn-time vs attach-later instrumentation. L2-10 hook the *reaction* (exit, alert, network report) rather than the check |
| **Tools** | Frida, frida-trace, objection, strongR-frida/fridare builds, LLDB, Ghidra, IDA, Hopper, Binary Ninja, radare2/r2frida |
| **Observe** | Does the RASP detect its own instrumentation (threads, D-Bus, anonymous exec memory, inline trampolines, GOT rebinding, swizzled IMPs, breakpoints)? Does a hooked check leave a trace (missing "started" bit, missing report)? Is there a single choke point (one boolean, one wrapper)? Does the server notice a missing or suspiciously clean report? |
| **Pass** | Generic and renamed instrumentation are both detected in-process; hooking a single function does not yield a clean, accepted report; reaction is server-side |
| **Fail** | One hook on one function (or one NOP) produces a clean verdict that the backend accepts |
| **Report** | "RASP defeated by hooking `<function>` (R2)". Map: MASVS-RESILIENCE-2 / -4; MASWE-0065 (dynamic analysis tools detection); legacy MASTG-TEST-0089 (anti-debugging), MASTG-TEST-0091 (RE tools detection) / MASTG-KNOW-0085, -0087; ATT&CK Mobile T1630 Indicator Removal on Host |

### What Kaisel provides today

| Adversary move | Kaisel check (code) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Force a boolean | **No boolean exists**; report is signals plus score | Design-level mitigation |
| Hook a check to return early | **`checkSuppressed` (1000, hard)** via started/completed bitmask; randomised check order | |
| Inline hooks (Frida, Substrate, ElleKit, Dobby) | `inlineHook` (300), `prologueChanged` (306), `syscallStubAltered` (304) on ~26 sensitive functions | PAC-stripped decoding |
| fishhook / interposing | `symbolRebound` (301), `symbolForeignImage` (305), `interposeSection` (204) | |
| Swizzling | `objcSwizzled` (303) | IMP image and prologue |
| Software breakpoints | `breakpointInstruction` (302) | |
| Debugger | `ptraced` (400), `codeSigningDebugged` (401, sticky), `exceptionPorts` (402), `parentProcessUnexpected` (403); optional `PT_DENY_ATTACH` | Raw syscalls |
| Frida | `fridaThread` (500), `fridaPortOpen` (501), `fridaDBus` (502), `anonymousExecutableMemory` (503, corroborated), `fridaMemoryMarker` (504) | |
| Late agent load | `imageAddedAtRuntime` (203) | Image-add callback |
| Hooks on the RASP itself | Self-prologue check | |
| Periodic re-checks | `IntegrityMonitor`, `monitorIntervalStarved` (1003) | |

### Gaps

- **Hardware breakpoints and watchpoints** are not detected directly (only `P_TRACED`, `CS_DEBUGGED`, ports, ppid).
- **Stalker / code-copy tracing** relies on anonymous exec memory, which is corroboration-only after the iOS 27 false positive.
- **Pre-baseline hooks**: anything hooked before Kaisel initialises is outside self-integrity (documented).
- **Renamed Frida with a clean D-Bus port** leaves only generic signals.
- **Manually mapped images** that never enter dyld's lists are only caught by anonymous-memory heuristics.
- **No in-process integrity of Kaisel's own `__TEXT`**; static patches are left to the server-side hash.

### Suggested tweaks (later)

**K5** debug-register check, **K6** Mach-O header scan of anonymous regions, **K7** Kaisel self-`__TEXT` hash, **K8** thread inventory delta, **K9** reaction decoupling (report-only by default).

---

## Layer 3: Tamper with the app before it runs

### What the adversary may do

Modify the package rather than the device. This is the dominant real-world path on **non-jailbroken** devices, especially on iOS 17+ where public kernel jailbreaks for A12+ are scarce.

| Technique | Detail | Public examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Decrypt** | Dump the FairPlay-decrypted binary from a jailbroken device (`cryptid` becomes 0) | frida-ios-dump, bagbak, flexdecrypt, Clutch (legacy) |
| **Inject a library** | Add an `LC_LOAD_DYLIB` for FridaGadget or a tweak, or drop a dylib into `Frameworks/` | insert_dylib, optool, objection `patchipa`, Sideloadly/ESign tweak injection, Azule |
| **Re-sign** | Free/paid developer, ad hoc, enterprise, or TrollStore with arbitrary entitlements | Xcode, codesign, ldid, zsign, AltStore/SideStore, TrollStore |
| **Entitlement abuse** | Grant `get-task-allow`, `no-sandbox`, `platform-application`, `skip-library-validation` | ldid, TrollStore |
| **Binary patch** | Remove or invert checks (Layer 2 applied statically) | Ghidra, Hopper |
| **Bundle manipulation** | Change bundle ID, strip `embedded.mobileprovision`, alter Info.plist (e.g. drop `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes`) | Manual repackaging |
| **Run elsewhere** | Run an iOS app on Apple Silicon macOS, in the simulator, or virtualised iOS | Corellium, Mac "Designed for iPad" |

### How pentesters assess it

| Item | Method |
|---|---|
| **Preconditions** | Decrypted IPA (or a test build provided unencrypted with the client's consent); signing identity; non-jailbroken device with Developer Mode; optionally TrollStore device on a supported version |
| **Test cases** | L3-1 re-sign unmodified with a different team. L3-2 inject FridaGadget via load command. L3-3 inject via `Frameworks/` with no load-command change (lazy load). L3-4 static patch of the check and re-sign. L3-5 ad hoc / enterprise / TrollStore install with crafted entitlements. L3-6 modified Info.plist. L3-7 run on Apple Silicon Mac or virtual device. L3-8 repackaged app talking to the production-like backend |
| **Tools** | frida-ios-dump, bagbak, insert_dylib, optool, objection `patchipa`, Sideloadly, AltStore/SideStore, TrollStore, zsign, ldid, codesign, ios-deploy, Xcode (NCC Group and WithSecure re-signing methodologies) |
| **Observe** | Does the app notice: team ID, provisioning profile, `get-task-allow`, `cryptid=0`, unexpected entitlements, load commands, extra frameworks, `__TEXT` hash? Critically: **does the backend notice** (App Attest assertion fails for a re-signed app)? |
| **Pass** | Repackaged builds are detected on-device *and* the backend refuses them because attestation cannot be produced or fails |
| **Fail** | A re-signed, instrumented build reaches authenticated APIs and performs sensitive actions |
| **Report** | "Repackaged and re-signed application accepted by backend (R1/R2)". Map: MASVS-RESILIENCE-2; legacy MASTG-TEST-0090 (file integrity), MASTG-KNOW-0086 / -0140, MASTG-KNOW-0090 (device binding); PCI DSS v4.0 Req. 6.2.4; ATT&CK Mobile T1406 Obfuscated Files or Information (defensive context) |

### What Kaisel provides today

| Adversary move | Kaisel check (code) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Decrypted binary | `binaryDecrypted` (603) | `app` domain, scored only when `expectedDistribution` set |
| Re-signed | `teamIdentifierMismatch` (901), `provisioningProfilePresent` (900), `enterpriseProfile` (903), `getTaskAllow` (601), `codeSignatureInvalid` (600) | |
| Crafted entitlements / TrollStore | `suspiciousEntitlement` (602) | List-based |
| Bundle manipulation | `bundleIdentifierMismatch` (902), `bundlePathUnexpected` (904) | |
| Injected library | `suspiciousImage` (200), `unexpectedImagePath` (201), `interposeSection` (204) | Allow-list includes the app's own bundle, so a renamed dylib inside `Frameworks/` is *allowed* |
| Static patch | `textModified` (610) / `textHashMismatch` (907) against a build-time `__TEXT` SHA-256 | Best compared server-side |
| Simulator | `simulator` (1002) | |
| Genuine-app proof | App Attest assertion in `seal()`; verified by the reference server | Fails for re-signed apps |

### Gaps

- **Dylibs inside the app's own bundle are trusted by location.** A Gadget renamed to look like a legitimate framework and loaded from `Frameworks/` passes the location allow-list.
- **No load-command manifest.** Inserted `LC_LOAD_DYLIB` entries are not compared against the build.
- **No App Store receipt / `AppTransaction` verification**, which would independently prove an Apple-distributed install.
- **Mac and virtual-device runs** are not explicitly detected (MASTG-KNOW-0135/-0136); App Attest is the backstop.

### Suggested tweaks (later)

**K10** build manifest of load commands and bundled frameworks, **K11** `AppTransaction` JWS verification server-side, **K12** Apple-Silicon-Mac and virtual-device signals.

---

## Layer 4: Compromise below the app

### What the adversary may do

Use kernel read/write to make the kernel itself return stock answers. Raw syscalls do not help when the kernel lies.

| Technique | Detail | Public examples |
|---|---|---|
| **vnode-level path hiding** | Filter lookups so selected paths do not exist for selected processes | vnodebypass, KernBypass (historic; per hekatos, iOS 12–14 era) |
| **Code-signing state falsification** | Patch what `csops` reports (`CS_VALID`, `CS_DEBUGGED`, entitlements) | Kernel patches in private tooling |
| **Process-flag falsification** | Clear `P_TRACED`, hide debug state from `sysctl` | |
| **Boot and mount state falsification** | Report sealed snapshot / read-only root while running a modified system | |
| **Trust cache and PPL abuse** | Run unsigned code as platform code without a classic injection footprint | Dopamine-family techniques (public write-ups) |
| **dyld or launchd patching** | Modify the loader once so every process inherits changes | Rootless jailbreak internals |
| **Userspace-reboot hygiene** | Mask evidence of the post-exploit userspace restart | |

### How pentesters assess it

| Item | Method |
|---|---|
| **Preconditions** | A jailbreak with kernel r/w on the test device; this layer is usually *time-boxed* or assessed by design review, because building kernel-level hiding is out of proportion for most engagements |
| **Test cases** | L4-1 public kernel-level hider where one exists for the version. L4-2 design review: which checks depend solely on kernel answers (filesystem, `csops`, `sysctl`)? L4-3 residual signals: what remains when all filesystem and code-signing answers are stock (images, threads, hooks, memory, timing, server history)? L4-4 boot-session continuity: does the server notice a device that was previously flagged? |
| **Tools** | Dopamine/palera1n kernel primitives (libkrw/libkernrw), vnodebypass/KernBypass where supported, Corellium for controlled kernel state |
| **Observe** | Which detections survive kernel falsification; whether the risk model treats absence of filesystem evidence as weak |
| **Pass** | Product documentation and server policy do not rely on on-device absence of evidence for high-risk actions; residual in-process signals and server history still fire |
| **Fail** | Backend treats "client says clean" as proof of a stock device |
| **Report** | Usually an **observation or design risk**, not a vulnerability: "client-side integrity cannot be trusted against kernel-level adversary; server relies on it for `<flow>`". Map: MASVS-RESILIENCE-1; MASTG-BEST-0029; ATT&CK Mobile T1404 Exploitation for Privilege Escalation |

### What Kaisel provides today

| Adversary move | Kaisel response |
|---|---|
| vnode hiding, `csops`/`sysctl` falsification | Acknowledged as **not observable** (DETECTION.md). Remaining: image lists, hooks, threads, entitlements, memory checks |
| dyld patching | `dyldModified` (110), indicator, iOS 15 only in practice |
| Userspace reboot | `userspaceReboot` (111), indicator |
| Earlier sighting | `previouslyJailbroken` (908), server per-key history |
| Design honesty | `PlatformCapabilities.notes` embedded in every report so the server knows what absence means |

### Gaps

- No kernel-behaviour probes beyond `userspacereboottime` and dyld comparison.
- No server-side boot-session tracking (boot time and userspace reboot time over time per key).
- Historic kernel-task-port exposures (`host_get_special_port` 4 / tfp0-style) are not probed. They are legacy, but cheap.

### Suggested tweaks (later)

**K13** kernel task port / special-port probe (legacy jailbreaks), **K14** server-side boot-session timeline per key, **K15** sysctl surface fingerprint (research item).

---

## Layer 5: Lie to the server

### What the adversary may do

Ignore the client entirely and attack the evidence path.

| Technique | Detail | Public examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Replay** | Capture a clean sealed report from a stock device and resend it | Generic API replay |
| **Forwarding / relay** | Keep a clean "donor" device that produces genuine reports on demand for a compromised device or bot | Fraud farms; Approov "how to defeat DeviceCheck/App Attest" |
| **Strip or downgrade** | Remove integrity fields, claim App Attest is unsupported, force the fallback path | HITB 2021 "Fooling Apple's AppAttest API" (Lyrchikov): drop the Apple connection, fake an old iOS version |
| **Sign a forged payload in-process** | Under Frida, call the genuine app's signing path with a forged report; assertion is valid because the key is genuine | Documented in Kaisel LIMITATIONS.md |
| **Key farming** | Generate many attested keys on few devices; reset after flagging | App Attest receipt risk metric exists for this |
| **Parser and policy abuse** | Exploit backend verification bugs (development AAGUID accepted in production, counter not enforced, RP ID not checked, nonce reuse) | HITB 2021 case 3 |
| **History reset** | Erase the device or delete the Keychain marker to clear "seen jailbroken" | |

### How pentesters assess it

| Item | Method |
|---|---|
| **Preconditions** | Intercepting proxy with certificate pinning bypassed on a test build; stock donor device; jailbroken device; test accounts; agreement that backend decisions are in scope |
| **Test cases** | L5-1 replay a sealed report (same nonce, then new nonce). L5-2 modify one signal and resend. L5-3 strip the assertion / send the "unsupported" path. L5-4 send a development-environment attestation to production. L5-5 counter rollback. L5-6 donor relay: genuine report from device A used for session on device B. L5-7 forged payload signed in-process under instrumentation. L5-8 previously flagged key sends a clean report. L5-9 erase and re-enrol. L5-10 high-risk transaction without fresh evidence |
| **Tools** | Burp Suite, mitmproxy, Frida (pinning bypass on test build), custom API client, App Attest verification checklist from Apple documentation |
| **Observe** | Server decision and reason codes for each case; whether evidence is bound to session, transaction and device key; whether failures fail closed |
| **Pass** | Every case except L5-7 is rejected or stepped-up; L5-7 is caught by corroborating runtime signals or behavioural fraud controls; L5-8 is raised |
| **Fail** | Server accepts replayed, stripped, modified or development-environment evidence, or fails open when attestation is absent |
| **Report** | These are usually **the highest-severity findings** because they are direct authorisation weaknesses. Map: MASVS-RESILIENCE-1/-2, MASVS-AUTH, MASTG-KNOW-0090 (device binding); PCI DSS v4.0 Req. 6.2.4, 8; CBUAE device-binding and fraud-control expectations; OWASP API Top 10 API2 (broken authentication) where relevant |

### What Kaisel provides today

| Adversary move | Kaisel response (`Server/`) |
|---|---|
| Replay | Single-use challenge, 300 s TTL, `clientDataHash == SHA256(payload‖nonce)` |
| Modify | Secure Enclave signature plus App Attest assertion over the canonical report |
| Strip / unsupported | Default `deny` without App Attest (`--no-require-app-attest` gives `step_up`) |
| Development attestation | AAGUID checked; development only with `--allow-development` |
| Counter rollback | Strictly increasing assertion counter |
| Client verdict trust | Server re-scores signals with its own weights and policy |
| Run once, then hide | Per-key jailbreak history raises later clean reports to `high` |
| History reset via HTTP | Deliberately no endpoint |

### Gaps

- **Donor relay and in-process forged signing** cannot be fully solved by attestation; there is no transaction binding beyond the session nonce and no behavioural layer.
- **App Attest receipt is stored but unused**: no fraud-risk-metric refresh to spot key farming.
- **DeviceCheck two bits not implemented** in the reference server (documented only).
- Reference server is single-process with no TLS or auth (documented as a teaching implementation).

### Suggested tweaks (later)

**K3** DeviceCheck bits, **K16** transaction-bound nonces, **K17** App Attest receipt risk metric, **K18** report-cadence and consistency checks.

---

## 8. Tiered test plan

Run each tier on both Kaisel apps (JB Check and Integrity Check/Attest) and, for client engagements, on the target app. Each tier keeps everything from the previous tier and adds one adversary capability.

| Tier | Name | Device state | Adversary capability | Layers | Expected Kaisel outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T0** | Stock baseline | Stock, latest and oldest supported iOS; Developer Mode on and off | None | — | `trusted`; no conclusive signals; record any false positive |
| **T1** | Stock jailbreak | Dopamine / palera1n, default bootstrap, package managers installed, tweak injection on | Visible artefacts | 1 | `compromised`: 105/100, 200, 905; rootful adds 103/104/102 |
| **T2** | Hiding tweak | T1 plus Shadow-class hider, then RootHide / Choicy per-app exclusion | libc hooks, then no injection | 1, 2 | Shadow-class: 108, 202, 300/301/303 expose the hider. Per-app exclusion: Suspicious on 1.3.1 from residual indicators (clean on 1.2); 908 / server history raise it further (lab-confirmed gap) |
| **T3** | Renamed artefacts | T2 plus renamed bootstrap, renamed package managers, renamed Frida (threads, ports, strings), Gadget renamed inside `Frameworks/` | Defeat name lists | 1, 2, 3 | Names fail; location, hook and memory checks should still fire (201, 300, 503 corroborated, 401/402). Renamed in-bundle Gadget is a known gap |
| **T4** | Behaviour-level hiding | Instrumentation that avoids patches (Stalker-style), hardware breakpoints, pre-baseline hooks, static patching, kernel-level hiding where available | Defeat behaviour checks | 2, 3, 4 | Partial; expect reliance on 1000, 401, 610/907 and server-side App Attest |
| **T5** | Server lies | Any of the above plus API manipulation | Evidence path attacks | 5 | Server rejects or steps up L5-1 to L5-6 and L5-8 |

**Per-tier procedure (summary):** prepare device state → install build under test → cold launch → collect report JSON ("Copy result JSON") → background/foreground once (monitor) → send to server → record decision → reset (Reset jailbreak history only when the test case needs it). Compare with `Lab/expected.json` using `Lab/compare_results.py`.

**Matrix minimum:** one rootless A12+ device (iOS 15/16), one checkm8 device (rootful and rootless), one stock device on the newest iOS, one non-jailbroken device for re-signed builds.

---

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

---

## 10. Reporting template

Use one finding per *bypass path*, not per check. Keep client data out; reference evidence by ID.

```markdown
## <ID>: <Title, e.g. "Jailbreak detection bypassed by per-app injection exclusion; backend accepts session">

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Severity | Critical / High / Medium / Low / Informational (CVSS v4.0 vector if required) |
| Bypass grade | R0–R4 (client), S held / not held (server) |
| Adversary layer | 1 Hide / 2 Hook / 3 Tamper / 4 Kernel / 5 Server |
| Tier reproduced | T1–T5 |
| Affected | App + version, backend endpoint(s), iOS versions |
| Preconditions | Device state, tools, account type |
| Effort | Time, skill, availability of public tooling |

**Description.** What the control is meant to do and what the adversary achieved, in two to four sentences.

**Observation.** What was observed (signals present/absent, client reaction, server decision). No weaponised steps; reference the evidence pack.

**Impact.** Business consequence: which sensitive flows (login, beneficiary add, transfer, card provisioning) were reachable from the untrusted environment.

**Root cause.** E.g. single local decision point; server does not require attestation; no device history.

**Recommendation.**
1. Short term: …
2. Structural: move enforcement server-side; require fresh attested evidence for high-risk actions; …

**Mapping.**
| Framework | Reference |
|---|---|
| OWASP MASVS v2 | MASVS-RESILIENCE-1 / -2 / -3 / -4 (and MASVS-AUTH where server-side) |
| OWASP MASTG / MASWE | e.g. MASTG-KNOW-0084, MASWE-0051, legacy MASTG-TEST-0088 |
| CBUAE | Applicable notice/clause per client's compliance register (see §12) |
| PCI DSS v4.0 | e.g. Req. 6.2.4 (software attacks), Req. 11.4 (penetration testing) |
| MITRE ATT&CK Mobile | e.g. T1628 Hide Artifacts |

**Evidence.** Report JSON IDs, screenshots, proxy history references.

**Retest.** Status and date.
```

**Severity guidance.** A client-side bypass with **server enforcement intact** is usually Low/Informational (defence-in-depth). A client-side bypass that **unlocks high-risk flows** because the server trusts the client is Medium/High. Any **server acceptance of replayed, stripped or forged evidence** is High/Critical in a banking context.

---

## 11. Proposed Kaisel tweaks

For the next implementation round. Effort: S (hours), M (days), L (week+). "Layer" is the adversary layer it addresses.

| ID | Layer | Tweak | Signal idea | Why | Effort | Risk of false positives |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **K1** | 1 | **Mount table enumeration** through a raw `getfsstat`, compared with a per-iOS allow-list of mount points, filesystem types and flags | `unexpectedMount` (indicator) | Overlays, bind mounts and tmpfs over system paths survive libc hiding; today only `/` is checked | M | Medium: allow-list per major iOS; validate on T0 |
| **K2** | 1, 4 | **Full code-signing flag profile** from `csops(CS_OPS_STATUS)`: expect `CS_HARD`, `CS_KILL`, `CS_RESTRICT`, `CS_ENFORCEMENT`, library validation; flag missing ones | `codeSigningFlagsRelaxed` | Jailbreaks commonly relax these for injected processes; cheap with the existing raw `csops` | S | Low–medium: record stock values on T0 for each distribution type |
| **K3** | 1, 4, 5 | **DeviceCheck two bits in the reference server** (set on conclusive evidence, query on each sensitive request) | Server reason `deviceCheckFlagged` | Survives reinstall, Keychain deletion and per-app hiding; today only documented | M | Low |
| **K4** | 1 | **MAC/sandbox policy probes**: operations a stock container sandbox denies with a specific errno (selected `mach-lookup`, sysctl writes, sandbox-extension consumption) | `sandboxPolicyRelaxed` | Jailbreak sandbox patches can relax policy without exposing files | M | Medium: research per iOS; keep as indicator |
| **K5** | 2 | **Debug-register check**: read `ARM_DEBUG_STATE64` for own threads; any enabled BVR/WVR is a hardware breakpoint or watchpoint | `hardwareBreakpoint` (hard) | Closes the hardware-breakpoint gap in LIMITATIONS.md | S | Low |
| **K6** | 2, 3 | **Mach-O header scan in anonymous executable regions** (magic, load commands) alongside 503 | `manualMappedImage` (hard when corroborated) | Catches Frida's own loader and images removed from dyld's `infoArray` | M | Low if limited to non-file-backed r-x regions |
| **K7** | 2, 3 | **Kaisel self-`__TEXT` hash** of `CIntegrityCore` against a value embedded at build time (post-link step) | `raspCoreModified` (hard) | Static NOPs in Kaisel itself are currently only visible to the server-side app hash | M | Low; must be computed after code signing in CI |
| **K8** | 2 | **Thread inventory delta**: baseline thread count/start routines at first evaluation; flag new threads whose start address is outside any image | `foreignThread` | Survives renamed Frida threads | S–M | Medium: system frameworks spawn threads; require out-of-image start address |
| **K9** | 2 | **Reaction decoupling**: guidance and API so host apps never block locally by default; report and let the server decide | Docs + `IntegrityGuard` default | Removes the local choke point testers hook first | S | None |
| **K10** | 3 | **Build manifest**: at build time record the main binary's load commands and the list and hashes of bundled frameworks; compare at runtime and send to server | `loadCommandInjected` (hard), `bundledImageUnexpected` | Fixes "trusted because it is in my bundle" for renamed Gadget | M | Low |
| **K11** | 3 | **`AppTransaction` (StoreKit 2) JWS** sent with the sealed report; server verifies the Apple-signed JWS, bundle ID and environment | Server reason `notAppStoreInstall` | Independent proof of Apple distribution for App Store builds | M | Low; not applicable to dev/ad hoc builds |
| **K12** | 3 | **Apple-Silicon-Mac and virtual-device signals** (`isiOSAppOnMac`, hardware model consistency, App Attest availability) | `runningOnMac`, `virtualDeviceSuspected` | MASTG-KNOW-0135/-0136 coverage | S | Low |
| **K13** | 4 | **Kernel special-port probe** (`host_get_special_port` 4, `task_for_pid(0)`) | `kernelTaskPortExposed` (conclusive) | Cheap legacy check for older jailbreaks that expose the kernel task port | S | Very low |
| **K14** | 4, 5 | **Server-side boot-session timeline** per key: boot time, userspace reboot time, uptime; flag userspace reboots with unchanged boot time across reports | Server reason `bootSessionAnomaly` | Corroborates 111 over time instead of per report | M | Medium: stock iOS can restart userspace |
| **K15** | 4 | **Sysctl surface fingerprint** (research): compare readable `kern.*`/`security.*` values with stock per iOS | `kernelStateUnusual` | Possible residual of kernel patching | L | High until validated; lab-only flag first |
| **K16** | 5 | **Transaction-bound nonces**: nonce derived from transaction hash for high-risk actions; evidence must be fresher than N seconds | Server policy | Limits donor relay and pre-recorded evidence | M | None |
| **K17** | 5 | **App Attest receipt risk metric**: periodically exchange the stored receipt with Apple and act on the attested-key count per device | Server reason `keyFarming` | Receipt is stored but unused today | M | Low |
| **K18** | 5 | **Report consistency checks** server-side: iOS version and model vs `PlatformCapabilities`, cadence (missing monitor reports), sudden "all clean" after history of signals | Server reasons | Catches forged in-process payloads that are too clean | M | Low–medium |

**Suggested order.** Quick wins first: **K5, K2, K13, K12, K9** (all S). Then the two that close lab-confirmed or documented gaps: **K10** (renamed in-bundle Gadget) and **K3** (DeviceCheck history). Then **K6, K7, K11, K16, K17**. Keep **K4, K14, K15** as lab research before they are scored.

---

## 12. Standards mapping cheat sheet

| Adversary layer | MASVS v2 | MASTG knowledge / best practice | MASWE | ATT&CK Mobile | Regulatory hooks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Hide | RESILIENCE-1 (platform integrity) | KNOW-0084 Jailbreak Detection; BEST-0029, BEST-0030 | MASWE-0051 | T1628 Hide Artifacts | CBUAE RASP/MTD and rooted/jailbroken device requirements |
| 2 Hook/patch | RESILIENCE-2 (anti-tampering), RESILIENCE-4 (anti-dynamic analysis) | KNOW-0085 Anti-Debugging, KNOW-0087 RE Tools Detection; BEST-0041, -0047, -0048, -0074 | MASWE-0065 | T1630 Indicator Removal on Host | PCI DSS v4.0 6.2.4 |
| 3 Tamper | RESILIENCE-2, RESILIENCE-3 (anti-static analysis) | KNOW-0086 Storage Integrity, KNOW-0140 Source Code Integrity, KNOW-0089 Obfuscation; BEST-0065, -0067 | MASWE-0051–0065 range | T1406 Obfuscated Files or Information | CBUAE tampered-binary detection; PCI DSS 6.2.4 |
| 4 Kernel | RESILIENCE-1 | KNOW-0084; KNOW-0135 Virtual Devices | — | T1404 Exploitation for Privilege Escalation | Design risk; residual-risk statement |
| 5 Server | RESILIENCE-1/-2 with MASVS-AUTH | KNOW-0090 Device Binding | — | — | CBUAE device binding, fraud controls, server-side evidence retention; PCI DSS 8 |

**Notes on mapping.**
- Legacy MASTG v1 test IDs (MASTG-TEST-0088 jailbreak detection, -0089 anti-debugging, -0090 file integrity, -0091 RE tools detection, -0092 emulator detection) are still widely cited in reports; MASTG v2 moves to KNOW / BEST / MASWE identifiers. Cite whichever the client's checklist uses.
- **CBUAE.** Vendor analyses report Notice No. 2176.2026 (April 2026) asking UAE banks for integrity checks at launch, rooted/jailbroken/tampered-binary detection, and MTD or RASP controls, plus off-device evidence retention ([Promon summary](https://promon.io/security-news/fighting-mobile-fraud-in-the-uae-what-bank-leaders-need-to-know-about-cbuae-notice-2176)); authentication guidelines are commonly cited as Notice 3057 ([Feroot summary](https://www.feroot.com/cbuae-notice-3057-march-2026-deadline/)). These are secondary sources: **map to the clause numbers in the client's own compliance register**, not to the vendor summaries.
- **PCI DSS v4.0.** Req. 6.2.4 (preventing common software attacks) and Req. 11.4 (penetration testing) are the usual anchors for mobile resilience findings in card-holder flows.

`docs/SERVER.md`:

```md
# Attestation and the reference server

App Attest, the Secure Enclave seal and how a backend should verify a sealed report.

## Is there an iOS equivalent of Play Integrity?

Not on the device. Play Integrity's verdicts are produced by Google's servers. The app only relays a signed token. The Apple equivalent has the same shape, and it is **server-side**:

| Android | iOS |
|---|---|
| Play Integrity `appIntegrity` (genuine, unmodified app) | **App Attest** (`DCAppAttestService`): a hardware-backed key whose attestation and assertions your server verifies against Apple's CA. A re-signed or modified app cannot produce valid assertions. |
| Play Integrity `deviceIntegrity` (`MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY`) | **No Apple equivalent.** Apple does not attest "this device is not jailbroken". App Attest proves a real Apple device and a genuine app, but it can still succeed on a jailbroken phone. Jailbreak status comes only from on-device checks such as this kit. |
| Device recall bits | **DeviceCheck** (`DCDevice`): two bits per device, stored by Apple, that you set from your server (for example "seen jailbroken"). |
| Root checker apps | `JailbreakDetector` / the Kaisel JB Check app. |

The robust design is therefore: run the on-device checks, seal the report with App Attest (`IntegrityKit.seal`), and let the server verify the assertion and decide. The client-side verdict is evidence, not proof.

## Reference server

```bash
cd Server
python3 -m venv .venv && . .venv/bin/activate && pip install -r requirements.txt
python3 -m unittest discover -s tests                # offline tests, synthetic Apple CA
curl -O https://www.apple.com/certificateauthority/Apple_App_Attestation_Root_CA.pem
python3 server.py --app-id ABCDE12345.com.dhanesh.kaisel.integritycheck \
  --root-ca Apple_App_Attestation_Root_CA.pem --allow-development --expected-distribution none
```

In Kaisel Integrity Attest, enter `http://<your-mac-ip>:8080`, tap **Enrol App Attest** once, then tap **Verify with server**. In Kaisel Integrity Check (free Apple ID) the enrol button is disabled and **Verify with server** sends a Secure Enclave-only seal, so start the server with `--mode lab` (or the older `--no-require-app-attest`, which now means lab mode). A lab-mode server never answers `allow` without App Attest. The response shows `decision`, `reasons`, the server's own device, runtime and app verdicts, and the App Attest and Secure Enclave status. App Attest needs a paid developer team (it is not available on a free personal team) and a real device. Without it the server answers `deny` (use `--no-require-app-attest` for `step_up` instead).

### Strict and lab modes (1.4.0)

| | `--mode strict` (default) | `--mode lab` |
|---|---|---|
| App Attest assertion | Required; missing or failed means `deny` | Missing means `step_up` |
| Report schema | `sdkVersion` 1.4.0 with `reportSchemaVersion` 2 | Also 1.3.x reports (no schema field), as `step_up` |
| Unknown finding code | `deny` | `step_up` |
| Malformed fields, tampered payload, wrong or reused challenge | `deny` | `deny` |
| Incomplete checks | `step_up` (never `allow`) | `step_up` |

Before scoring, `policy.validate_report()` checks the structure: a supported SDK and schema; a signals
array whose codes are integers, confidences are 0 to 100 and details are strings; `checksCompleted` and
`checksExpected` as 32-bit unsigned integers. The **expected check set comes from the server's own SDK
profile** (`policy.SDK_PROFILES`), never from the client: a client that reports fewer expected checks, or
completion bits outside the profile, is denied. A missing check is incomplete coverage (`step_up`), and
`checkSuppressed` alongside a complete bitmap is a contradiction (`deny`). Every response carries
`mode`, `validation.action` and `validation.reasons`.

**Limitation.** Completion bits are reported by the app. App Attest proves which genuine app instance
signed the report and that the payload was not changed afterwards; it does not prove that the
measurements made inside a possibly compromised process are true. A complete bitmap is necessary for
`allow`, never sufficient on its own.

### Remembering jailbroken devices

The reference server applies the same jailbreak rule as the apps: conclusive evidence in any domain sets
the device verdict to `compromised` and lists it in `device.jailbreakEvidence`. It also keeps a
per-key history in its store (`_history`). Since 1.4.0 it is keyed only by an **App Attest-verified key**
(`aa:<keyId>`). A Secure Enclave key is chosen by the client, so an unbound Secure Enclave signature is
reported as `signature valid (unbound, not authentication)` and can neither create nor clear history. A
1.3.x entry keyed by a Secure Enclave key is still honoured when that key is bound to the verified App
Attest instance.
When a key that once sent conclusive evidence later sends a clean report, for example after the app was
hidden with RootHide, the device verdict is raised to at least `high` and the reason says when the jailbreak
was first seen.

For production, also use **DeviceCheck**: call `IntegrityKit.deviceCheckToken()`, and from your server set
one of the two per-device bits (`update_two_bits`) when conclusive evidence arrives. The bits are stored by
Apple per device and per developer team, survive app deletion and hiding, and are reset only when you
reset them. Query them (`query_two_bits`) on every sensitive request.

Entries are written only from conclusive evidence, and entries whose evidence is no longer conclusive
under the current rule are ignored (this discards entries created by the 1.3.0 dyld false positive and,
since 1.4.0, image entries that no longer classify as a known jailbreak injector).
To wipe the history, start the server with `--clear-history`. There is deliberately no HTTP endpoint for
this, because a client must not be able to erase its own record.

## Server-side verification

1. Issue a single-use nonce with a short TTL, bound to the session.
2. Check that `clientDataHash == SHA256(payload || nonce)`.
3. Verify the App Attest assertion with the key from enrolment. The counter must strictly increase and the RP ID must match `<TeamID>.<BundleID>`. No assertion, or a failed assertion, means the report did not come from your genuine app: treat it as `compromised`.
4. Verify the Secure Enclave signature with the public key enrolled for that device.
5. Re-score `payload.signals` under the server's own weights, and apply the policy per transaction risk (step-up, block, limit, or allow and flag).
6. Optionally store a per-device DeviceCheck bit ("seen compromised") so that a reinstall does not reset it.
7. Compare `facts.textSHA256` with the value your CI recorded for that build.

```

`docs/SIDELOADING.md`:

```md
# Sideloading with Impactor or Sideloadly

Sideloading installs an IPA onto your iPhone or iPad using your own Apple ID, without Xcode.
Both Kaisel apps work with a **free** Apple ID.

> Use an Apple ID you control. Sideloading tools send your Apple ID credentials to Apple to create a
> signing certificate; consider a secondary Apple ID rather than your main one.

## 1. Get the IPA

- **From GitHub Releases:** download `KaiselJBCheck-<version>-unsigned.ipa` and/or
  `KaiselIntegrityCheck-<version>-unsigned.ipa`, and `SHA256SUMS.txt`, from the
  [Releases page](https://github.com/0xdhanesh/Kaisel/releases). Check the hash:
  `shasum -a 256 -c SHA256SUMS.txt` (macOS) or `Get-FileHash <file> -Algorithm SHA256` (Windows).
- **Or build it yourself** on a Mac: `Scripts/build_unsigned_ipa.sh` (see [Building](BUILDING.md#option-c-unsigned-ipa-for-sideloading-tools)).

`KaiselIntegrityCheckAttest` is not distributed as an IPA: App Attest needs a paid team's provisioning
profile, which a sideloading tool cannot create.

## 2a. Install with Impactor

[Impactor](https://github.com/khcrysalis/impactor) is an open-source sideloading tool for macOS, Windows and Linux.

1. Download and install Impactor from its GitHub releases page.
2. Connect the iPhone by USB, unlock it and tap **Trust This Computer**.
3. Open Impactor and sign in with your Apple ID.
4. Select the device, choose the Kaisel IPA and start the install (button labels vary between Impactor versions; see its quick start).
5. Continue with [step 3](#3-first-launch-on-the-phone).

## 2b. Install with Sideloadly

1. Install [Sideloadly](https://sideloadly.io) (macOS or Windows; on Windows, install iTunes and iCloud from Apple's website, not the Microsoft Store).
2. Connect the iPhone by USB and trust the computer.
3. Drag the IPA into Sideloadly, enter your Apple ID and click **Start**.
4. Leave the bundle ID unchanged unless the install fails with an "already in use" error.

## 3. First launch on the phone

1. **Developer Mode (iOS 16 or later):** Settings › Privacy & Security › Developer Mode › On, then restart and confirm.
2. **Trust the developer:** Settings › General › VPN & Device Management › your Apple ID › **Trust**.
3. Open **Kaisel JB Check** or **Kaisel Integrity Check**.

## Free Apple ID limits

| Limit | Value |
|---|---|
| App lifetime | **7 days**. After that the app will not open; reinstall (re-sign) it. Paid accounts get one year. |
| Sideloaded apps per device | 3 active apps signed by the same free Apple ID |
| New App IDs | 10 per 7 days |
| Capabilities | No App Attest, push notifications or other paid-only entitlements |

Your data in the app is kept when you re-sign with the same Apple ID and bundle ID.

## How sideloading affects Kaisel's results

- **Kaisel JB Check** is not affected. It ignores how the app itself was signed.
- **Kaisel Integrity Check** shows the build as `development` (re-signed, `get-task-allow`, not FairPlay encrypted)
  and Developer Mode as on. These appear under **Informational** and do not change the **device** verdict.
  They are only scored if you set `expectedDistribution: .appStore` in your own app.
- Server verification works with a Secure Enclave seal only; start the reference server with
  `--no-require-app-attest` (see [Server](SERVER.md#reference-server)).

## Troubleshooting

| Symptom | Fix |
|---|---|
| "Untrusted Developer" | Trust the profile in VPN & Device Management (step 3.2). |
| App opens and closes immediately | The 7-day signature expired, or Developer Mode is off. Re-sign and check both. |
| "Maximum number of apps" | Remove another sideloaded app signed by the same Apple ID. |
| Install fails with a bundle ID error | Let the tool change the bundle ID, or build with your own `--bundle-prefix`. |
| Two-factor prompt never arrives | Sign in on the tool again and accept the prompt on a trusted Apple device. |

```

`docs/V1.4.0-VALIDATION.md`:

```md
# Kaisel 1.4.0 validation

Status: **unreleased**. The code changes are complete and unit-tested. Release gates that need real
devices are open. Do not tag 1.4.0 until every gate below is ticked.

## What 1.4.0 changes

| Area | Change | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Image classification | Explicit classes in `rasp_classify.c`: jailbreak injector (200, conclusive), instrumentation (205), trusted, marker (206), unknown external (201) | Broad markers (`crane`, `liberty`, `choicy`, ...) matched anywhere in a path were treated as conclusive injectors |
| Conclusive rule | Explicit allow-list of codes; 200 re-validated from its path. Replaces "not Frida means injector" | Same semantics in JB Check, Integrity Check, Keychain history and the server |
| Shared manifest | `Server/classification.json` feeds the C lists, the server and the parity tests | Stops the Swift, C and Python copies drifting |
| Remote rules | Image markers produce 206 only | Signed rules must not create a conclusive verdict |
| Server validation | Strict mode by default: supported SDK and schema, typed fields, server-side expected check set, no unknown codes, App Attest required | A report with incomplete checks or no attestation could be allowed |
| Server history | Keyed by App Attest-verified key only | A self-chosen Secure Enclave key is not device identity |

Full list: [CHANGELOG](../CHANGELOG.md). Defects, files and test output: [IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md](../IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md).

## Acceptance tests (spec P0.1)

All run by the shared fixtures (`Tests/Fixtures/image_classification.json`) in C, Python and Swift, and by
`Server/tests/test_classification.py` through server scoring.

| Input | Required | 1.4.0 |
|---|---|---|
| `CraneBank.app` with `CraneBank.framework` | No conclusive jailbreak from the name | trusted (1.3.1: conclusive) |
| `LibertyFinance.framework` | Same | trusted (1.3.1: conclusive) |
| `ChoicyAnalytics.framework` | Same | trusted (1.3.1: conclusive) |
| `roothideinit.dylib` in `.jbroot-*` | Conclusive | jailbreak injector |
| Substrate / ElleKit / TweakLoader in a jailbreak root | Preserved | jailbreak injector |
| FridaGadget in a repackaged app | Runtime, not jailbreak | instrumentation (205) |
| Unknown non-system image | Indicator | unknown external (201) |
| `/var/App.app2/...` with bundle `/var/App.app` | Not inside the bundle | unknown external |
| Marker from signed remote rules | Never conclusive | marker (206) |

## Acceptance tests (spec P0.2)

`Server/tests/test_validation.py`. In strict mode none of these is `allow`: empty report, `checksCompleted`
0, `checksExpected` 0, a client-reduced expected set, missing check bits, unknown completion bits,
invalid bitmap types (string, float, bool, null, negative, over 32 bits, array), unsupported SDK or schema,
legacy 1.3.x report, unknown finding code, malformed signals, tampered payload, tampered App Attest
assertion, wrong or reused challenge, missing App Attest assertion, unbound Secure Enclave signature only.
Also tested: a valid complete report is `allow`; development exceptions (`--expected-distribution
development --debugger-expected`) allow an Xcode debug build; indicators alone give `step_up`; history is
written and read only for an App Attest-verified key; stale broad-marker history is ignored.

## Test results

Recorded in [IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md](../IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md#tests-executed), with the date and the
environment each suite ran in. A suite that did not run is listed as NOT RUN, never as passed.

## Release gates

| Gate | Status |
|---|---|
| All confirmed P0 code changes implemented | Done |
| Swift, C and Python classification semantics consistent | Done (34 shared cases agree in C and Python; ImageClassificationTests pass in Swift) |
| Benign framework-name regression tests pass | Done (C, Python, Swift) |
| Server strict-mode incomplete-report tests pass | Done |
| Existing automated tests pass | Done (server 64, lab 11, Swift 27) |
| Both applications build | Done (all three schemes, unsigned Release, Xcode 27.2) |
| Available real-device scenarios have exported evidence | **Open.** No JSON exported; 1.4.0 not installed on any device |
| Unavailable device scenarios are explicitly NOT TESTED | Done ([Lab/COVERAGE.md](../Lab/COVERAGE.md)) |
| Documentation matches recorded observations | Done |
| No new regression on previously tested stock devices | **Open.** Re-run `stock` on iPhone18,1 / iOS 27.2.1 and the iOS 26 device with 1.4.0 |
| Remaining risks and limitations recorded | Done ([LIMITATIONS.md](LIMITATIONS.md)) |

## Device runs needed before tagging

1. `stock` on iPhone18,1 / iOS 27.2.1 and `stock_ios26`, both apps, JSON exported. Any conclusive finding blocks the release.
2. `dopamine_roothide` (expect PASS with 200 for `roothideinit.dylib`).
3. `dopamine_roothide_hidden_fresh` and `dopamine_roothide_hidden_seen` (expected miss and expected 908 respectively).
4. `trollstore`, if the device is still available.

Run `python3 Lab/compare_results.py --coverage --require-metadata Lab/results/*.json` and update
[Lab/COVERAGE.md](../Lab/COVERAGE.md).
```
